19ca291 2026-09-17 The parametric task's target is now standardized per voxel across the training runs instead of by one pooled scalar: GalerkinTransformer.density_scale is replaced by density_voxel_mean/density_voxel_scale (arrays shaped (query_row_count, 1), the decoder's own row-per-query-point layout), built by report.py's Perovskite_Voxel_Statistics from operators.deep_operator_network's own Pointwise_Statistics (already reused by residual_correction, now declared in this package's manifest.toml too) over the training runs only; GalerkinTransformer.Unstandardized_Target inverts it at inference before Conserving, and raises if handed a query row count the statistics were not measured on, since un-standardization is grid-bound to the gate's own angle-stratum resolution even though the network's own standardized-space output still answers any query grid. This is the canon's own §A.4-prescribed fix for this corpus (dynamic range ~420x, cusp voxels dominating a plain mean squared error), diagnosed directly on the training population: the top 5% of voxels by value carry 77% of the total sum of squares, which a pooled scalar standard deviation cannot rebalance since a uniform rescale preserves every voxel's relative contribution to the loss exactly -- the reason the previous commit's scalar density_scale fix alone plateaued 15-16x worse than the training-mean-field floor.
Two new tests pin it: a pure round trip of Pointwise_Statistics's own arithmetic including a zero-spread voxel staying finite, and the member's own Unstandardized_Target round-tripping and refusing a mismatched query count.

2b6809f 2026-09-16 The probe sampler mixes uniform and atom-centered draws, de-biased by inverse-proposal weights, with trilinear grid targets
Half the probes land uniformly in the cell, half as an isotropic gaussian offset (0.7 angstrom)
around a random atom; the proposal's own cartesian density (periodic images summed by cell
height) gives each probe a self-normalized importance weight, so the weighted mean over a batch
is an unbiased estimate of the uniform-cell average regardless of the mixture's own scale.
Trilinear interpolation of the stored grid is periodic (wraps at the boundary). ProbeBatchSource
and CachedDeepDftStructure hold this for training: atoms padded to the batch's widest structure,
magnetization masked out per structure where the field is absent or its grid-integrated absolute
moment is too small for the loss's own normalization to be meaningful.

c5ab62e 2026-09-16 The structure loader keys every atom by element and pseudopotential title, the composition chains atom-atom then atom-probe message passing, and the per-probe head reads density and magnetization
The framework's ExplicitStack is grid-typed and cannot host point-set message passing, so
MessagePassingStack is member-local: three atom-atom ContinuousDisplacementKernel layers, then
three atom-probe layers over one joined point set with roles, probes filtered to receive-only
through Sending_Points. The species loader resolves each atom's OUTCAR title by its own second
token (suffix stripped), routing anything unresolved to a member-local unknown key; SpeciesKeys
later maps any pair the training vocabulary never saw onto that same row. The readout is a
member-local two-layer perceptron (PointwiseProjection assumes a channel-first grid layout, not
PointSet's point-first one). Renormalization to the electron count is also member-local: the
shared Conserving wrapper flattens every channel together, which is wrong once magnetization
rides beside density, so this scales the density channel alone.

runs, which no starting scale alone can hold against. The report's stability-escalation paragraph

than knobs, the integrator's own to schedule.

db9d764 2026-09-12 Fix a real bug in the cap-hit logging just landed: it counted validation solves as training ones
The closure has no call-context argument, so it was folding every held-out unit's own solves into
the same window as the training steps' solves and triggering on total accumulated solve count
rather than on the training step count the instruction actually asked for ("at every validation
interval, the fraction of the last hundred training solves"). LocalizationBatches draws exactly
one example per training step (Next_Batch always returns a single example under a new leading
axis), while a validation pass stacks every example a held-out unit carries, so example_count == 1
is a reliable signal for "this solve came from a training step" for every unit that itself holds
more than one example -- the one honestly remaining gap, noted in the new comment, is a held-out
unit that happens to carry exactly one example, which would still be counted once per validation
pass. Caught before the corrected relaunch got past its first hundred steps.

69ab491 2026-09-12 The fixed point's tolerance is relative to its iterate, so it means the same thing on a toy grid and on the card's
The solver compared an absolute residual norm against the tolerance, which on a 64-channel 40-cubed field of four million entries made 1e-3 a relative tolerance of a few parts per million: every solve of the first real run hit its iteration cap while the toys converged in a dozen steps. The residual and the adjoint loop's change are now divided by the norm of the iterate they belong to, the recorded residuals are relative, and the canon's health floor reads as it was written.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>

7d359b9 2026-09-12 The fixed point injects its input into every iteration, without which its equilibrium was the same for every input
The solver iterated the shared layer on the state alone, seeding the iteration with the input and then forgetting it, as any contraction must: the equilibrium was a property of the weights, not of the input, and the first real run on the card sat at the target field's own variance because the member could only emit a constant. The deep-equilibrium form is a fixed point of the layer with the input added before the activation at every iteration. The solver, the phantom reentries and the implicit rule now inject it, the implicit rule carries the input as a differentiated argument so the parts upstream can train through it, and the weight-tied composition gains the same injection as an option, off by default, so the rung already trained keeps its meaning while the fixed point's exact unrolled counterpart exists. The audit's unroll ground truths inject; the equilibrium is proven to move with the input and the gradient to reach it under both rules.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>

d491fcd 2026-09-12 The fixed-point solver's residuals stay on their engine, and only the scalars the host decides on come across
On the card the solver was host-bound: every iteration copied the iterate and its image to the host, took the residual norm there, and fed whole residual histories into the Anderson least squares, which left the card idle for most of a step. The residual norm and the Anderson step reduce to inner products, so they are taken on whichever engine holds the residuals and only the norm, the small gram matrix and its right-hand side cross to the host; the condition check moves to the gram matrix, whose condition number is the square of the differences' own. The implicit rule's convergence check takes the same path. The mixing weights agree with the host computation to round-off across engines.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>

0043fef 2026-09-12 task="parametric" joins localization and potential as a third shape of the same factory, per the corrected design: one class, one __call__, no union and no second class. The input genuinely is a GridFunction -- Parametric_Input broadcasts a Coefficients parameter vector as constant channels over whatever grid it is given, that grid's own lattice riding along as the field's domain, exactly the way the canon's own phrase ("parameters broadcast as constant channels into the I.1 backbone") describes it. Forward_From_Coarse_Input appends periodic coordinate features of the input's own spatial shape before the lift, computed fresh at whatever shape is running, which is what makes the resolution-invariant query literal rather than aspirational: the same weights answer any grid because the coordinate channels are rebuilt for it, not cached for one.
The electron count rides in the condition argument every __call__ already carries and neither other task ever used -- exactly the seam Conserving(law="renormalize_to_electron_count") was built for. Predict_Field(parameters, output_discretization, electron_count) is the three-line convenience the design asked for: it builds the constant field, wraps the electron count as a one-entry Coefficients, and calls __call__ -- not a second path, and __call__ itself still refuses to run the parametric task without a condition rather than silently skipping the conservation law.

Lift width is six parameters plus PeriodicCoordinateFeatures' own count (25 at the shared default), metric-blind, since no density field ever enters this task for a kernel to be aware of. Tested directly on the production class: the answer integrates to the requested electron count under the grid's own quadrature, the same trained weights answer an 8-cubed and a 10-cubed grid alike, and __call__ raises rather than skips when no electron count is given. caa2d84 2026-09-12 The parametric variant's arm foundation lands, host-only, as report-local data per the standing instruction not to create a split artifact myself. An arm is one strain family or one perovskite stratum; a level is that arm's own swept parameter vector, one to three components depending on the family (uniaxial/biaxial/isotropic/one-angle-shear sweep one value; two-angle-shear sweeps two; triaxial and three-angle-shear sweep three, confirmed against the real census as clean or near-clean rectangular grids, not single-factor lines). Bracket_Corners generalizes "interpolates between bracketing parameter values" to any dimension: for each axis it finds the nearest smaller and larger value seen anywhere in the arm, then requires every one of the 2^D corner combinations to be an actual level in the data, which correctly refuses a bracket across a grid hole rather than assuming a complete factorial design.
Perovskite reads the same way once the real data is checked: the length and angle strata are not six independent single-factor sweeps -- a, b and c (respectively alpha, beta and gamma) vary together on a shared 5-value grid, 112 of 124 length runs and 112 of 125 angle runs deviating on more than one factor at once. So perovskite gets the identical two three-dimensional arms, reusing Bracket_Corners rather than a separate one-factor scheme; the existing 0.8/1.2 extrapolation holdouts fall out for free as points with no smaller or larger neighbor on some axis, with no need to special-case them.

The design trap flagged before building anything is tested directly: Test_A_Constant_Only_Input_Can_Only_Answer_A_Constant_Field proves a spectral-plus-pointwise stack fed nothing but constant channels stays constant to round-off, and Test_Coordinate_Features_Break_The_Constant_Output_Degeneracy_And_Answer_Any_Grid proves periodic coordinate features (Fractional_Grid_Coordinates into PeriodicCoordinateFeatures, reshaped back to channel-first) break that and let the same weights answer an 8-cubed and a 12-cubed grid alike -- the resolution-invariant query the parametric member's own operator character rests on. Both run on a minimal lift/spectral/project stack built directly from the framework's own parts, ahead of wiring task="parametric" onto FactorizedFourier itself.

Every run-to-arm-and-level assignment is tested against the real pool: totality (every run lands exactly once), and bracket integrity (every corner an interior level names is itself a real level of that same arm) for all seven strain families and both perovskite strata, with counts bounded against the real numbers measured directly off the census (423 interior strain levels, 45 interior perovskite levels). Full gate green: 388 passed.

9add0e0 2026-09-12 Softmax shifts by its own row mean before exponentiating, so a large logit cannot overflow
exp of an unshifted logit overflows single precision near 88, and the learned
temperature can walk scores there over a long run. Subtracting each row's mean
logit over the source tokens leaves the softmax exactly unchanged (it is
shift-invariant) while bounding the exponent by the row's spread rather than
its level. Factored into its own Softmax_Over_Last_Axis so Attention_Weights
composes it rather than repeating the exponentiate-and-normalize inline.

4cb6916 2026-09-11 The energy trunk lands, completing the member, and beats the ridge floor the headline metric hides
DeepOperatorNetwork gains its fourth and last configuration: Energy_Trunk_Network assembles a branch
(SensorEncoder, 7->256->256->128) over the six strain components plus the functional as a seventh
numeric feature, read against a BasisExpansion trunk (9->128->128->128->128) over the one energy
coordinate through RampedCoordinateFeatures rather than the periodic map the position trunk uses,
because energy has no far face to wrap onto. The member closes with a softplus head: DeepOperatorNetwork
now carries a NONNEGATIVE_HEAD_CONFIGURATION check that applies Softplus after the trunk's inner product
in both Forward_Point_Values (the lifted, differentiable path the loss trains through) and __call__ (the
plain-numpy path report figures and inference use), leaving the other three configurations' forward
untouched. The member itself now publishes last_predicted_curve through a new Inspect() override, the
softplus head's own output at the last query, because this configuration's readout is a pre-activation
and not the physical quantity by itself the way the other three's readouts already are.

Trained pooled: both functionals share one member rather than the one-per-functional split canonical
uses, because the card carries functional as a branch feature rather than a partition. The whole training
role (1,856 curves, 30 orbits, under 5 MB) is one fixed batch rather than point-sampled, because the
aligned energy grid is identical for every run and a point sampler has no varying shape to earn its cost
against here; the trunk's own features are computed once on the window rescaled to minus one to one and
held as an engine constant for the whole run. The loss is the card's own curve_l1 made differentiable, as
each run's own L1 residual normalized by that run's own curve size and averaged over runs, not a
standardized MSE the way the other configurations train. A three-stage schedule (2e-3, 7e-4, 2e-4 over
800, 1,200 and 5,000 steps) was chosen after 1e-2 was measured to overshoot within the first validation
passes and never recover; the final stage was still improving at its last step, and extending it from
2,000 to 5,000 steps (2.5x the compute) moved the whole-window median by 5.1% and the band-edge median by
1.9% against a ridge floor already cleared by over 40% at the shorter budget, so the boundary is recorded
in report.md rather than chased further.

Measured on the committed holdout (1,856 train, 256 validation, 248 test over 30 orbits): the member
beats the ridge floor by 44.0% on the whole -28..+8 eV window and 48.9% on the -2..+6 eV band-edge region
where the strain signal actually concentrates, and beats the training-mean floor by 47.5% and 65.0% on
the same two windows. No kill margin is invented: VI.1 fixes none, and report.md states the measured
skill against both floors rather than writing pass or kill. A second, clearly labeled run trains the same
architecture on a band-edge-weighted ablation of the loss (5x inside -2..+6 eV) and is reported beside the
card-loss row, not in place of it.

gap_edge_error surfaced a real, worth-recording finding rather than a bug: both floors read a near-zero
gap error and the member reads 1.9125, and grounding it directly shows every one of the 248 test truths
crosses one percent of its own peak at exactly +0.02 eV with zero variance across every strain family --
the coarse smearing bridges the sharp valence edge into a shoulder that crosses the threshold long before
the true conduction band starts, for any curve shaped like a real one, so a floor built from real curves
inherits that shoulder and reads near zero by sharing the artifact rather than by finding the gap. The
member's smoother, low-order trunk cannot fall back to exact zero the way a sharp, smeared feature does,
so it clears the threshold further out; this is exactly why test-suite.md already calls the support-edge
read-out a diagnostic only, next to the trusted occupancy-walk gap, and report.md records the mechanism
rather than the bare number. The band-edge-weighted ablation's own gap_edge_error lands back near zero,
confirming the mechanism: weighing that region five times over is enough to reproduce the shoulder.

configs/energy_trunk.toml's window_eV was wrong (predated the loader's own measurement) and is corrected
to (-28.0, 8.0) with the reasoning recorded beside it, matching canonical.toml's convention of a recorded
starting point beside the code's own constants. IMPLEMENTATION.md gains paragraphs on the three choices
that distinguish this configuration from canonical, on its no-kill-margin standing, and on its inspection
surface. The existing 126 figures are untouched (confirmed by git status showing only the new
figures/pooled/energy_trunk/ directory, 22 files, 18 from the inspection suite plus best/worst prediction
curves and error spreads by family and by functional) because their own training code was never
re-invoked this session; regenerating them was skipped deliberately rather than risking GPU-nondeterministic
drift against a byte-identical baseline that a fresh full run cannot actually guarantee.

Eight new tests cover the factory, the ramped (non-periodic) feature map, the non-negative head under
deliberately extreme inputs, a fixed-basis sibling's absence of the same guard, the point-sampled gradient
(both on NumpyEngine and, skip-guarded, on the card through Training_Engine), querying between the aligned
grid's own points for a finite non-negative curve, and the captured curve reachable through Inspect().
343 passed (335 baseline plus 8 here), pyright strict clean, naming and comment gates clean, the
torch-seam scan confirms report.py and __init__.py never name the foreign engine.

e5e8d65 2026-09-11 The implicit rule stops raising, and the ladder's third rung now has all four audit columns measured against each other rather than two.
Vector_Jacobian_Product lands in operators/substrate/custom_gradient.py, which this stream now owns outright: on the foreign engine it detaches the point, re-enters it as a fresh leaf, and asks torch.autograd.grad for the cotangent pulled back through function's Jacobian there; on the reference engine it takes the central difference of the scalar the cotangent's own dot product with output forms, so the primitive never raises off the foreign engine and stays oracle-checkable the way every other substrate facet is. Exported through the root beside CustomGradient.

Wiring it into the implicit rule's adjoint surfaced a second real gap past the one already flagged: a declared backward runs with the ambient tape off, confirmed by forcing a small reproduction under torch.no_grad and watching a freshly-detached, requires-grad-true leaf still produce an output with no grad_fn. torch.autograd.grad inside Vector_Jacobian_Product's foreign path was therefore differentiating nothing every time it ran inside Bridge.backward, silently, no exception. The fix is one torch.enable_grad() block inside Vector_Jacobian_Product itself, local to that one function, needed because a hand-declared backward is exactly the one place in this package where reaching into the engine's own autodiff from inside another autodiff pass is the point.

Implicit_Resolved flattens the shared layer's named parameters into one fixed order, wraps CustomGradient around the existing Solved(), and its backward solves (I - J)^T v = g by the same kind of fixed-point iteration the forward already uses, each step a Vector_Jacobian_Product against the equilibrium; once v stops moving, one more Vector_Jacobian_Product per named parameter -- each holding every other parameter fixed at its saved value -- gives the gradient CustomGradient's contract asks for, in argument order. Checked against dense (I - J) built by brute-force finite differences on a grid small enough to invert directly, the iterative adjoint agreed to 1.3e-12; checked against forward-mode times reverse-mode on the actual eight-cubed layer via the standard adjoint dot-product identity, to 3e-10. The mechanism was right from the first correct run.

Getting a correct-looking wrong answer out of a right mechanism took a detour worth recording. The audit's own contractive layer left local_linear's bias at PointwiseLift's zero default, which makes the origin an exact fixed point of the whole layer -- a linear kernel sends zero to zero, GELU(0) is zero -- and for a genuine contraction that fixed point is unique, so the true dz*/dtheta for every weight but the bias is not small, it is exactly zero, independent of theta. Finite differences through the reference engine and the weight-tied full unroll do not see that: both measure the sensitivity of Solved()'s fixed, three-iteration stop from a theta-independent start, which is a real but different quantity from the true implicit derivative, and does not collapse the same way. Three of four gradients looked wrong against that ground truth by two to three orders of magnitude before this was understood; the fourth, the bias itself, agreed throughout, because it is the one direction where the degenerate case and the true gradient coincide. Giving local_linear a nonzero bias removes the degeneracy the audit was never supposed to be testing, and every column agreed at once.

On the audit's own seeded layer the solve converges in 3 iterations to residual 6.9e-04. The two ground truths agree with each other to a relative gap of 2.2e-04. Phantom at depth one sits 2.48e-02 from both, its reported bias; at depth three it closes to 2.5e-05 against finite differences and 2.2e-04 against the full unroll. Implicit closes to 4.0e-06 against finite differences and 2.2e-04 against the full unroll -- the same distance the two ground truths sit from each other, which is what an exact adjoint is supposed to do and a truncated one is not.

The vector-Jacobian-product primitive gets its own oracle test first: a linear map's Jacobian is the matrix itself, so Vector_Jacobian_Product's result on both engines is checked against that closed form to 1e-8, not just against each other. Two more tests exercise the implicit rule's numpy path directly, since CustomGradient never reaches a declared backward there at all.

326 passed (302 baseline plus 24 here), pyright strict clean, naming and comment gates clean, torch-seam scan confirms fixed_point.py still never names the foreign engine even with the adjoint now built.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

c62633f 2026-09-11 Detached lands in substrate, and the fixed-point rung's phantom column now has a measured, bounded bias against both audit ground truths rather than an engine it could not yet touch.
Rebased onto neural-operator-suite at f32059c to pick up the kernels stream's Roll_Along_Axes and Contract_Channel_Axis and the multi-scale stream's composition, both landed on trunk since the last commit here. Detached is appended after the kernels stream's additions in operations.py, one line exported from substrate's root, exactly the primitive proposed and approved: value.detach() where the value is foreign, the value itself where it already is not.

Wiring it in exposed a real correctness question the earlier numpy-only version never had to answer: Anderson's mixing step computes weights on the host from flattened residual differences, and mixing those weights back into the field naively -- state = sum(weight * applied_value, ...) -- pulled every applied_value from a list of numpy-converted arrays, which would have forced the iterate off the foreign engine the moment acceleration kicked in, stranding it there while the layer's own weights stayed on the tape. Einstein_Summation and the rest of the dispatch in substrate/fourier.py decide which engine to use by inspecting their own two arguments, so a numpy iterate meeting foreign-engine weights on the next application does not quietly fall back to a slower path, it hits torch.einsum with a raw ndarray and raises. The fix keeps applied_history holding whatever the layer actually produced, detached but native to its own engine, and only ever converts to numpy for the small host-side least-squares itself; mixing multiplies by plain host floats, which both engines accept without coercion. Every iterate is detached the moment Applied_Once produces it, which is what makes the solve tape-off in fact and not only at its two endpoints.

The mandatory audit's first half now runs for real. On the seeded 8-cubed contractive layer the three tests share, the solve converges in 3 iterations, and its two ground truths -- finite differences through the reference engine on the converged solve, and a weight-tied full unroll at that same depth-3, tape on throughout -- agree with each other to a relative gap of 1.5e-04, which is the audit's own self-consistency check passing before either is trusted against phantom. Phantom at its default depth of one sits 2.24% from both ground truths: a real, bounded, reported bias, not the near-exact match its truncation is not supposed to produce. Phantom at depth three closes to under 0.01% against both, confirming the bias is a truncation artifact that more reentry removes rather than a defect in the reentry mechanism itself. jacobian_free, being phantom truncated to one reentry by construction, inherits the depth-one number exactly.

Two new primitive tests stand beside the audit: Detached severs gradient on the foreign engine while a control path in the same loss keeps carrying one, and is a proven identity on the reference engine where there is no tape to cut. Anderson_Mixing_Weights gets its own direct tests too, now that a value worth checking exists: it declines a synthetic near-parallel residual history outright (condition number past the ceiling, restart rather than an unstable mix) and its weights sum to one on an ordinary one.

implicit still raises NotImplementedError. The vector-Jacobian-product primitive its adjoint needs is approved and scoped to substrate/custom_gradient.py, which this stream now owns outright, but is not built in this commit -- this is the ladder's third rung with its solver and two of its three backward rules complete and audited, its exact adjoint still to come in a second commit.

323 passed (302 baseline plus 21 here), pyright strict clean, naming and comment gates clean, the torch-seam scan confirms fixed_point.py still never names the foreign engine.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

ec309d3 2026-09-11 The ladder gains its weight-tied rung whole, and its fixed-point rung as far as the two backward rules that need no custom gradient at all.
WeightTied is ExplicitStack's shape with the per-layer indexing removed: one Layer applied depth times, kernel and local linear weights collected once under kernel./local_linear. rather than once per application, gradients accumulating across every reuse because nothing detaches between them. Its own foreign-engine test drives a three-deep tied stack through both engines and finds every gradient agreeing with central differences (loss values match to 8e-09, every one of the shared array's entries within 1e-4 relative), which is the control the canon asks for: the suite can now say "weight-tying yes, DEQ no" if that is where the evidence points, because the rung exists standalone.

FixedPoint applies that same one Layer under damped Picard, Anderson-accelerated once two residuals exist: an m-1 by m-1 Tikhonov-regularized normal-equations solve (differences of the flattened residual history against its most recent entry, regularization 1e-4, restart on a condition number past 1e8) mixing the F-values rather than the iterates, tolerance 1e-3 and cap 32 exactly as specified. On an 8-cubed contractive layer it reaches residual 2.8e-04 in 3 iterations where plain damped Picard on the identical layer takes 13 -- the acceleration is doing real work, not just adding machinery. A layer scaled 1000x larger hits the cap honestly: finite output, last_cap_was_hit true, last_iterations_taken 32, nothing silently wrong. Every one of these numbers -- iterations, final residual, the cap flag, the full residual-norm trace -- comes back through Inspect() as a named array, not just as an attribute on the object.

Of the three backward rules, phantom and jacobian_free are built and work today: detach at the equilibrium, then reenter the plain lifted path for one application (jacobian_free) or phantom_depth applications (phantom, default 1, canon ceiling 3). Concretely jacobian_free is phantom truncated to depth 1 -- their outputs are identical to 1e-12 on every test case -- so there is one reentry mechanism, not two, and jacobian_free is simply phantom's cheapest setting. implicit is not built. Its adjoint needs (d f/d z)^T v at the equilibrium every iteration of its own fixed-point solve, and f is a generic Layer with no closed form to hand-derive -- that has to come from the foreign engine's own autodiff, which CustomGradient's plain-Python backward callable has no way to reach. Selecting backward="implicit" raises NotImplementedError naming this rather than guessing.

The bigger gap is upstream of implicit specifically: nothing in substrate can detach a value from the tape. Phantom and jacobian_free's "detach at z*" step, and the canon's own requirement that the Picard/Anderson solve itself run with the tape off, both need it, and there is no way to get it from inside this file without either the banned raw foreign-engine access or a substrate primitive that does not exist yet -- I checked every module in operators/substrate/ before concluding this and flagged it to the team lead rather than working around it, per the house rule. Concretely this means the solve and the backward-rule dispatch are correct and fully tested on the numpy path (Apply, and Forward called with numpy lifted values, which is what every test in this commit exercises), but calling Forward with foreign-engine tensors that require grad -- what the mandatory gradient audit needs for phantom -- fails today at the first numpy conversion inside the solve, honestly rather than silently. The audit as specified (phantom vs IFT vs finite differences vs full unroll) needs that primitive for phantom and needs a second, larger one (a vector-Jacobian-product, or equivalent access to the foreign engine's own autodiff from inside a hand-written backward) for implicit. Neither exists in this worktree.

What is verified instead, honestly short of the full four-way audit: finite differences through the reference engine on the converged solve (the one ground truth that owes nothing to any tape) show both weight groups are genuinely live at the equilibrium -- kernel gradient 3.8e-06, local_linear gradient 26.5, both far above the ~5e-10 noise floor a 1e-6 central-difference step leaves. And the solver's own equilibrium agrees with a weight-tied unroll run ten iterations past the depth the solver took, confirming the fixed point Solved() reports is a genuine attracting point of repeated application and not an artifact of stopping early.

Fourteen new tests, all failing before this change since WeightTied and FixedPoint did not exist. 296 passed in 81.34s, up from the 282 baseline, pyright strict clean, the naming and comment gates clean, and the torch-seam scan confirms fixed_point.py never names the foreign engine.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

f32059c 2026-09-11 The multi-scale composition's U shape exists now, descending into a bottom and back up through skip connections
MultiScale in operators/compositions/multi_scale.py holds three groups of Layer
objects -- a descending tuple, one bottom layer, and an ascending tuple -- plus
an output_scale index fixed at construction. Descent runs each scale's layer
then halves the field into the next; the bottom layer runs once with no
further descent; ascent doubles the bottom's output back up, concatenates it
on the channel axis with the matching descending scale's own saved output, and
runs that scale's ascending layer, stopping the moment output_scale is reached
rather than climbing all the way back to the finest grid. Parameter_Values and
Inspect both walk the same three groups under descending_N / bottom /
ascending_N prefixes, so a stack of any depth names every array without
collision, and Apply records per-scale output norms and shapes for Inspect to
expose.

The resampling had to be built fresh rather than reused: framework/invariance.py's
Spectral_Truncation_Resample calls np.fft.fftn directly and casts to float64,
so lifting a stack through it would sever the tape the moment the engine is
the differentiable one. Spectral_Resampled instead calls Real_Fourier_Transform_3d
from the substrate, which already dispatches per engine, then truncates or
zero-pads the two full spectral axes and the one half-stored axis by the same
gather-the-kept-modes, zero-fill-the-rest construction SpectralKernel's own
Gathered_Modes and Placed_Modes already use for its mode weights -- read
before writing this, not reinvented. Downsampled_By_Two and Upsampled_By_Two
wrap it at exactly a factor of two, and Halved_Shape raises rather than rounds
when an extent will not split evenly, which is what a 15-cube input is for.

Layer_Applied raises the identical NotImplementedError -- same string, same
position after the kernel-plus-local sum and before the activation -- that
ExplicitStack.Layer_Outputs already raises on activation == "alias_free", so
the fused kernel the convolutional entry still owes drops into an
already-wired slot without this composition guessing at its gradient rule.

Seven tests, all failing at collection before this change since MultiScale did
not exist. The one the entry's whole claim rests on builds a field from only
the low integer-cycle modes a coarser grid still resolves and drives it
through two downsamples and two upsamples on a sixteen-cube: the round trip
lands within 5.3e-15 of the original, machine noise. A second test zeros just
the concatenated skip contribution going into one ascending layer, holding the
bottom's own path fixed, and shows the layer's output moves. A third confirms
Forward and Apply agree on the same numbers. A fourth -- torch-gated -- drives
an 886-parameter U with a real skip connection through both engines and finds
every kernel and local-linear array gets a nonzero gradient agreeing with
central differences to 5.3e-08 absolute at worst. Full suite: 289 passed, up
from the 282 baseline, pyright strict and the torch seam scan included.

155a145 2026-09-11 The tabulated stencil's forward stops severing the tape, and the low-rank kernel gets a lifted forward of its own
A survey of the five kernels found exactly one, SpectralKernel, with a Forward that
stayed on whichever engine lifted it. TabulatedStencilKernel's Forward opened with
np.asarray(input_values, ...) and closed every contribution through np.roll and
np.tensordot -- three numpy-only calls standing between any weight and a gradient.
LowRankKernel had no Forward at all: Kernel_Matrix and Integrate did the whole
computation in plain numpy, with nothing engine-agnostic to lift through.

TabulatedStencilKernel needed two things neither numpy nor torch share a name for:
a periodic roll along the three spatial axes, and a channel-mixing block contracted
against a value's leading channel axis. Both join Sum_Over_Last_Axis and
Concatenate_Channels in operators/substrate/operations.py as Roll_Along_Axes and
Contract_Channel_Axis, dispatched on Is_Engine_Native exactly like their neighbors,
exported through the substrate root. tabulated.py now imports only those two names
from operators.substrate and never mentions the foreign engine itself, so the raw
torch scan holds even though the kernel package is not exempt from it. Forward
gained the output_shape argument LiftedKernel asks for; since this kernel is
tabulated for one grid and cannot answer for another, Forward is where that check
now lives, raising the exact message Integrate used to raise inline, and Integrate
becomes the numpy wrapper the house pattern expects -- asarray the input, call
Forward, asarray the output.

LowRankKernel turned out not to need either new primitive. Its only learned array
is core; the feature maps are fixed, not learned, and the contraction connecting
them -- two matrix products and a transpose -- is already engine-agnostic through
plain @ and .T, the same way DenseKernel's Forward always was. So Forward takes
the two feature matrices and the quadrature-weighted source values as arguments,
already lifted, and contracts them against lifted["core"]. Building those three
constants needs Source_Points_And_Values, Quadrature_Weights and Output_Points,
none of which know about an engine, so a new Lifted_Constants(engine, input_function,
output_discretization) does what FixedModeExpansion's own Lifted_Constants does in
operators/readouts: compute the fixed part in numpy, then lift it once. Kernel_Matrix
stays, now feeding last_kernel_values from the same targets and sources Integrate
already computed rather than duplicating that lookup inline. LowRankKernel is
Kernel[Representation, Coefficients], not grid-shaped, so it was never asked to
satisfy LiftedKernel and does not -- ExplicitStack calls layer.kernel.Forward with
one array and a grid shape, and this kernel's Forward wants two feature matrices
and a weighted-value array instead. A composition that wanted to train it inside a
stack would need its own Layer-shaped container keyed to Representation rather than
GridFunction, and would have to call Lifted_Constants once per forward -- the feature
matrices are fixed by the query, not by core, so a caller evaluating the same points
repeatedly could hoist that call out of the training loop entirely.

Neither protocol was touched. TabulatedStencilKernel satisfies LiftedKernel
structurally now, the same way it already satisfied Kernel nominally, and pyright
strict is what actually checks that -- confirmed by reverting just the four source
files under a stash and watching the assignment to a LiftedKernel-typed variable
fail type-checking while the rest of the new tests failed at collection or call
time with AttributeError and TypeError instead. All thirteen new tests fail against
the prior code, twelve of them at pytest and one only under pyright strict.

Both kernels: Forward and Integrate agree to 1e-12, the lifted path matches
Dense_Reference_Integral exactly where Integrate already did, and gradients reach
the one learned array each has. stencil_weights' largest gradient is 114.4, agreeing
with central differences to within 9.8e-09 absolute at worst, loss values matching
to 2.8e-14; core's largest gradient is 14.5, agreeing to within 1.3e-09 absolute,
loss values matching to 0.0. 295 passed, up from 282 baseline, pyright strict and
the torch seam scan included.

67b2fe3 2026-09-11 A layer's local linear term becomes a learned part, and the stack trains through both halves of its own equation now
ExplicitStack.Apply wrapped every layer's output in np.asarray(...) and called
layer.kernel.Integrate, the numpy-only inference path, regardless of whether the
kernel underneath had a lifted Forward to offer instead. The gradient tape was
severed at the composition boundary on every stack, unconditionally. The other
half of the same defect sat in Layer itself: local_linear was typed as a bare
Callable[[Array], Array], an opaque function with no parameter_values, so the
W*v(x) term of the layer's defining equation had never been trainable at all --
the one existing test built a stack with a hardcoded halving function, because
nothing else was possible.

Fixing the kernel half meant touching the Kernel protocol, which is exactly the
trap the brief warned about. Grepping every class that inherits Kernel for
Forward turns up SpectralKernel, TabulatedStencilKernel, ContinuousDisplacementKernel
and DenseKernel with one, and LowRankKernel with none. But having a Forward
is not the same as having a compatible one: SpectralKernel's takes a third
output_shape argument because it changes grid resolution, TabulatedStencilKernel's
does not and cannot, since it is only ever tabulated for the grid it was built on,
and ContinuousDisplacementKernel's Forward is a five-argument message-passing form
built around its own periodic radius graph, unrelated to a grid forward in shape
or purpose. No single signature covers more than one of these, so nothing was
added to the shared protocol. A second, sharper trap turned up along the way:
Kernel.supported_representations is a ClassVar, and pyright checks ClassVar
members invariantly under structural protocol matching, which is a different and
stricter rule than the one applied to a class's own declared nominal bases.
A protocol that inherits Kernel wholesale -- even only to add one method --
breaks conformance for every kernel that only nominally inherits the original,
SpectralKernel included, the moment something is checked against it structurally
instead of nominally. LiftedKernel therefore does not inherit Kernel; it redeclares
Integrate by hand beside a new Forward, which costs nothing since every kernel
that actually has a lifted Forward already has Integrate too. Right now that
protocol has exactly one satisfying member, SpectralKernel, which is what the
one existing test already used and what a spectral-layer member would need.
TabulatedStencilKernel and LowRankKernel are both left outside it and are exactly
the two kernels this stream is reporting back as needing their own lifted path.

The local_linear half is now LocalLinearMap, a protocol asking for Forward,
parameter_values and Inspect -- precisely what PointwiseLift already is, read
in operators/encoders before writing this. Reusing PointwiseLift's type directly
was the other option on the table and the one rejected: encoders already imports
from framework, so framework importing PointwiseLift back would be a cycle, and
a protocol is what Kernel already uses to solve the identical problem.

ExplicitStack.Forward composes layer.kernel.Forward and layer.local_linear.Forward
under one shared lifted dict, keyed layer_N.kernel.* and layer_N.local_linear.*
so that two layers built from the same kernel class never collide, and sliced
back to each part's own unprefixed names before either Forward is called --
the same naming scheme Inspect already used, now shared with Parameter_Values,
the new method that hands a trainer the whole stack. Apply becomes the numpy
wrapper around Forward, exactly the MultilayerPerceptron-shaped two-path idiom,
built over a shared Layer_Outputs so that last_layer_norms keeps its per-layer
granularity without a second copy of the composition's math.

Three new rows and one adapted one. Two confirm Forward and Apply agree on the
same input and that a two-layer stack's collected names are distinct and complete.
The fourth drives a residual two-layer stack through both engines: gradients
reach all eight arrays across two kernels and two local linear maps, loss values
match to 0.0 and every gradient agrees with central differences to within 4.3e-09
absolute (1.5e-06 relative) at worst. All four fail against the prior code --
three on AttributeError or TypeError, the adapted one because PointwiseLift is
not the callable Layer used to accept. 275 passed, against 272 before, pyright
strict and the torch seam scan included.

b9badc2 2026-09-11 The canary reaches the card, and its numbers move exactly once and say why
Every published number of the branch-trunk member had been produced on the host
in double precision, because report.py constructed the engine with no arguments
and that was the default. The trainer has selected a device by house word since
this morning; this is the one commit that lets the report use it, held back so
the committed figures and tables would move once rather than twice.

Two things changed together. The report now carries a configuration axis: each
functional is measured under principal_component on the raw fields and under
proper_orthogonal on fields standardized voxel by voxel before the
decomposition, each on its own basis, with figures nested one level deeper. And
the engine is the accelerator in single precision. The candidate budgets and the
search that chooses among them are untouched on purpose, so the only difference
from the committed principal_component rows is the arithmetic.

That makes the diff on this commit a measurement, and it came out cleaner than
expected. On the accurate functional, where the chosen budget was 32000 both
times, the number moved from 0.001013 to 0.001092 -- up 7.8 percent -- against
a seed spread this morning's twelve-run sweep measured at 35 percent. On the
cheap functional it moved from 0.001069 to 0.000821, down 23 percent, with the
budget also doubling, against a spread of 17 percent. Opposite directions, both
inside the noise. Changing the arithmetic is indistinguishable from changing
the seed. There is no precision correction to apply to old numbers; there is
only the rule that comparisons live inside one arithmetic.

The same run checked itself twice. The regenerated principal_component rows
reproduce the sweep's seed-zero cells to six digits, 0.000821 and 0.001092, from
a different script under the same arithmetic, budget and seed. And
proper_orthogonal lands at 0.000819 and 0.001122 against the sweep's 0.000817
and 0.001112; the gap is the learned offset, which the report leaves at zero
where the sweep fit it in closed form, and it is a hundred times below the
noise, exactly the size it was measured to be.

The report says of itself what it must: every row is one training run, the
sweep is the finding, and the ordering of any two rows here is not. Eight of
eight floor comparisons pass, all four blocks chose the top of the budget range
and the caveat records it, and the figure suite is 78 files across both
configurations, drawn from cached inspection arrays rather than a live retrain.

6c541da 2026-09-11 The readout that breaks the linear-reconstruction ceiling exists now, latent and position read jointly
NonlinearDecoder concatenates a latent row with a point's own coordinate features and reads the
pair through one MultilayerPerceptron, so the field value at a point is a genuine nonlinear
function of both together. BasisExpansion's trunk-times-branch product is linear in the latent by
construction -- this is the named alternative the design record calls for, and the readout
abstraction absorbs it without a seam: no integral, no linear combination, just Forward(lifted,
inputs) reused whole.

Concatenating a per-point feature matrix against a single latent row with no point axis needs a
broadcast, and an engine-agnostic Forward cannot reach for a numpy-specific or engine-specific
tile call to get one. Checked directly against the installed engine rather than assumed: a raw
numpy array added to a lifted tensor raises TypeError, "concatenation operation is not implemented
for NumPy arrays." So the point features are lifted once per query through a Lifted_Constants
method, mirroring the fixed-basis readouts' own pattern for a constant that must ride along in a
graph. Inside Forward, a zero-multiplied slice of that already-lifted matrix broadcasts one
channel's latent row onto every point through plain addition, and the join lands on the feature
axis by transposing through Concatenate_Channels -- which only ever concatenates rows -- rather
than hand-rolling a second concatenation primitive in a package this stream does not own.

Multiple channels share the one network and loop over their own latent rows; the output shape
matches BasisExpansion's convention exactly, point axis first, so __call__'s grid-or-point tail
could be copied rather than re-derived.

Seven tests, all failing at collection (ImportError, since none of this existed) before this
change. The one that matters most evaluates both readouts at fixed points for latents a, b and a
plus b, mean-removed: BasisExpansion lands within 6.7e-16 of additivity, exactly linear to
numerical noise, while NonlinearDecoder departs by 0.097, comfortably past the 1e-3 floor -- one
test, one shared method, proving the distinction rather than asserting it twice. The rest confirm
a grid query and the same points as a PointSpec agree to 1e-12, the direct Forward call matches
the inference path, channel splitting matches independent single-channel decoders built from the
same seed, a miscounted latent is refused rather than broadcast, Inspect exposes the network's own
layers plus the last query's point features under the grid or point shape it was asked in, and
-- torch-gated -- gradients reach all four of the network's arrays with the torch and reference
engines agreeing on both value and gradient to 1e-10.

Full suite: 279 passed, up from the 272 baseline, pyright strict and the torch seam scan included.

9a745c5 2026-09-11 A parameter the loss never reaches comes back as zero rather than as a crash
This was predicted in writing before the engine had ever executed a line --
"Gradients reads tensor.grad unguarded, which is None for any parameter the loss
does not touch" -- and then sat unhit for a month because every loss written so
far happened to touch everything it was handed. The encoders stream hit it
building a part whose conditioning weights are used only when a covariate is
supplied, which is exactly the shape that finds it: a leaf the graph never
reached keeps no gradient at all, and reading .grad.detach() off it raises
AttributeError from inside the engine.

Zero is not a fallback here, it is the answer. A loss that does not depend on a
parameter has derivative zero with respect to it, so the engine now returns an
array of zeros shaped like the parameter rather than refusing. The reference
engine already agreed: central differences perturb an untouched parameter and
see the loss not move, so it has always returned zeros on this path and only the
foreign engine crashed. The new row is parametrized over both, which is what
makes that agreement a checked property rather than a coincidence -- and it
fails on the foreign engine alone before this change.

The other half of this commit is a convention the encoders stream established
and correctly refused to document in files it did not own. An atom embedding
keyed by element alone conflates pseudopotential variants, and twelve elements
ship two of them across these campaigns, so the key is the pair. PointSet has no
title field and gaining one would widen the framework for a single consumer, so
the pair rides on species itself as two columns. That is a real change to what a
documented field means, and it now says so in the framework and in the readme
table rather than only in the part that assumed it.

272 rows pass, against 270 before.

a689261 2026-09-11 A voxel that never moves is guarded rather than divided by
Pointwise standardization divides each voxel by its own spread across the
training block, and nothing computed that spread with a floor under it. On the
strain atlas the hazard is latent rather than live -- measured, the quietest
voxel still carries a spread of 7.1e-03 against a median of 3.8e-02, a mild
dynamic range of seventeen and not one exact zero on either functional. So
nothing already measured is affected.

It does not stay latent. This configuration is not strain-only forever, and the
campaigns it will meet next are exactly the ones with quiet regions: perovskite
spans a dynamic range of 420 against diamond's 21, and the defect and alloy
blocks carry genuine vacuum. A voxel with no spread there divides by zero and
the readout returns a field of infinities with nothing raised anywhere -- a
silent corruption, which is the class of failure a green suite cannot see.

Pointwise_Statistics computes both statistics in one place with the guard the
package already uses on the same hazard: Fit_Standardized_Ridge floors its own
parameter spreads at one for exactly this reason, and this follows it rather
than inventing a second convention. The new row pins a training block whose
first voxel never moves and asserts the readout stays finite through it.

f64eb66 2026-09-11 The second fixed-basis configuration exists now, and what actually sets it apart took two corrections to find
The design record split proper_orthogonal from principal_component on three counts: fixed modes
plus the training mean, a basis fit on training folds only, and an explicit learned bias. The first
two never were differences. FixedModeExpansion already adds the training mean back on every call,
so that was never new. And both configurations fit Gram_Pod on training-fold-only data by the
identical call -- confirmed empirically rather than by reading, because a rank-32 ceiling computed
independently here reproduces report.md's committed 0.000017 to all six digits before this
configuration's own basis is even touched.

The bias survived that first check -- FixedModeExpansion carries no such array, its
parameter_values is {} -- so BiasedModeExpansion was built to carry one, and a first pass of
Proper_Orthogonal_Network assembled the member around it. It measures as a structural zero.
Gram_Pod centers on the mean, so the reconstruction residual against the same training block a
basis was fit on sums to exactly zero at any rank, standardized fields or not -- verified both
analytically and against the real corpus. The published bias cannot distinguish these two
configurations in this codebase. BiasedModeExpansion and output_bias are kept regardless: faithful
to the published form, one scalar, and still part of the configuration's actual readout below --
but nothing is claimed for them, and the measured offsets (0.0006-ish on both functionals, against
a real trained branch rather than an exact projection) bear that out.

The one piece of the published form the member genuinely lacked is pointwise output
standardization: each voxel's own mean and spread across the training runs, applied before
Gram_Pod runs rather than after. Decision D5 chose global per-campaign standardization instead,
for a stated reason -- pointwise statistics do not exist across the varying grids the canonical
configuration spans. proper_orthogonal and principal_component both live on a fixed, same-shape
block by construction, exactly where that reason evaporates. PointwiseStandardizedExpansion, in
operators/readouts, carries this: the basis is fit on standardized fields, the learned offset is
added in that same standardized space, and only then is the whole thing carried back onto the
training block's own scale, voxel by voxel.

Proper_Orthogonal_Network assembles it beside Principal_Component_Network, taking the training
block's per-voxel mean and spread the way it already takes the basis -- computed once by the
caller, not inside the factory. Every array the member holds or learns is reachable through
Inspect() in this same commit: the modes and the offset it shares in spirit with the plain biased
expansion, and now readout.voxel_mean and readout.voxel_scale as fields of their own.

Seven tests. Three exercise the readouts standalone: one shows the offset is purely additive on
the plain fixed expansion, one shows the pointwise readout matches its published formula exactly
(modes, standardized mean and offset combined, then carried back by scale and mean), and one shows
a per-voxel spread is not a global rescaling in disguise -- two readouts differing only in whether
their spread is uniform must disagree on the same input. The rest exercise the assembled member:
the offset collected beside the branch's arrays and nothing else, both it and the pointwise
statistics inspectable under the readout prefix, a field of the requested shape out, and gradients
reaching the offset through a field-space loss built from the readout's own Forward. All seven
fail at collection (ImportError, since none of this existed) before the change and pass after.
Full suite: 250 passed, up from the 243 baseline, pyright strict and the torch seam scan included.

Measured the way principal_component's own report is measured: same split, same rank-32 basis (now
fit on standardized fields), same branch architecture, same step-count search, orbit-aggregated
medians on held-out test. Against principal_component's committed 0.001069 (cheap) and 0.001013
(accurate): proper_orthogonal reaches 0.000817 on cheap, 23.6% better, and 0.001087 on accurate,
7.3% worse. Both clear the ridge floor by a wide margin -- 80.3% and 73.5% against the 25%
requirement -- and the representation's own rank-32 ceiling in the standardized space (0.000018,
0.000021) lands almost exactly where the raw-field ceiling already did, so none of the movement is
the representation losing capacity. Pointwise standardization is a real, measured difference from
principal_component in a way the bias never was: it moves the number in both directions, winning
one functional and losing the other. That split is recorded as measured, not resolved toward
either verdict -- three negative findings would have been informative and this is not that, but
neither is it a clean win, and the honest report is the split itself.

4e2f24f 2026-09-11 Two encoders land, each keyed by the identity a single field cannot carry
AtomEmbedding gives deep_dft the encoder its manifest already named: atoms
in, atoms carrying a learned feature vector out. The table is indexed by
the pair (element, pseudopotential title) rather than the element alone,
because twelve elements in this corpus ship under two pseudopotential
variants across campaigns and the element alone would silently conflate
incompatible references. The vocabulary is explicit and ordered at
construction, so the table's rows are stable across runs rather than
resting on dict order, and a pair the vocabulary has never seen raises
instead of borrowing a row — the only way the compound key actually
prevents the conflation it exists to prevent. The title itself never
touches this module: it is assumed already resolved by whatever reads the
OUTCAR TITEL echo, and no POTCAR is opened, quoted, or referenced anywhere
here. Since PointSet carries no dedicated title field, the pair rides on
species itself, each atom's row holding its element symbol beside its
pseudopotential title, so the encoder's only per-atom input stays the one
field already named for identity.

VariableEncoding gives codomain_attention the encoder its manifest already
named: one learned encoding per channel label, gathered from an explicit
ordered vocabulary of the corpus's physical field names, lifted through
weights shared identically across every token. That sharing is the whole
premise — it is what lets the channel count run five to eight across the
corpus without the architecture caring which five or which eight showed up
in a given run. The functional covariate, when present, projects into that
same per-token encoding rather than arriving as an extra channel, so the
token count never grows for it. An unknown channel label raises rather than
falling back to any row.

Both parts follow PointwiseLift's two-path shape: an engine-agnostic
Forward touching only a lifted dict and dunders, and a numpy __call__ that
also records what Inspect returns. Eleven tests join PointwiseLift's and
SensorEncoder's in test_parts.py: shape and assembly, the raise on an
unseen key for each part, numeric agreement between the two paths to
1e-12, gradient flow into every learned array checked against NumpyEngine's
finite differences and cross-checked on TorchEngine (including that a
vocabulary row no call touches gets exactly zero gradient, not a leaked
one), and the property VariableEncoding's whole entry rests on: a channel's
encoding comes out identical whether or not other channels rode along with
it in the same call. All eleven were confirmed to fail on import with the
implementation stashed out.

257 rows pass, against 246 before.

82d0b59 2026-09-11 The engine seam can carry a custom backward, proven right by the engine that ignores it
operators/README.md already named two consumers waiting on this: the
fixed-point composition differentiates its solve by the implicit-function
adjoint rather than unrolling the iteration, and the alias-free activation
must fuse an upsample, nonlinearity and downsample under one custom gradient
rule because naive automatic differentiation on the doubled grid exhausts
the resident card, a correctness-of-scale requirement rather than an
optimization. Engine had no member either could reach for this: Evaluate,
Gradients, Value_And_Gradients and Lift_Constant cover a whole forward's
differentiation and one constant's entry onto the engine, nothing that lets
one node inside a forward declare its own backward.

custom_gradient.py adds CustomGradient, a frozen dataclass pairing a forward
over a tuple of positional arguments with the backward that stands in for
the chain rule through it, declared at the call site with no engine
reference in scope, matching how Evaluate and Gradients thread only lifted
data into a forward callable and never the engine itself. Apply dispatches
on Is_Engine_Native exactly as Exponential and the other operations in
operations.py already do: plain arguments call the declared forward
directly, and the declared backward is never reached at all. Native
arguments route through Applied_Through_The_Foreign_Engine, which asks
Torch_Module() for the foreign package only once a native tensor has
already proven it is installed, and binds a Bridge subclass of its
autograd.Function per call, saving the raw arguments and the output through
save_for_backward and returning the declared backward's own tuple of
gradients unchanged from Bridge.backward.

Engine itself is untouched. A method on it could not have reached this
problem: nothing in the composition, kernel or readout call chain that
eventually runs inside a forward carries an engine reference, only the
lifted arrays themselves, so the seam has to dispatch on what a value is
rather than on what engine produced it. This is also what makes the
reference engine's half free: NumpyEngine.Gradients already reruns the
whole forward from scratch at each perturbed parameter value and never
calls backward on anything, so a forward that happens to contain a
CustomGradient.Apply call is differentiated numerically through its true,
undeclared behavior with no code path written for it at all. The declared
backward is not skipped by a special case; there is no special case, which
is the whole point.

Five tests land in test_substrate.py.
Test_The_Reference_Engine_Never_Reaches_The_Declared_Backward wires a
backward that raises AssertionError into a plain-array call and confirms it
never fires. Test_A_Correct_Custom_Backward_Agrees_With_Finite_Differences
cubes a parameter under a correct declared backward and finds the foreign
engine's gradient within 4.8e-9 of the reference engine's central
difference and exactly equal to the closed-form derivative at value 1.7.
Test_A_Wrong_Custom_Backward_Is_Caught_Against_The_Reference drops the
correct backward's leading factor of three and finds the foreign engine's
gradient off by 35.69, exactly two-thirds of the true gradient's own
magnitude and far past any numerical noise floor.
Test_Gradients_Reach_A_Parameter_Standing_Before_A_Custom_Rule parametrizes
across both engines and confirms an ordinary rescaling placed before the
custom node still carries a correct gradient back to the parameter behind
it, matching the closed-form derivative exactly on the foreign engine and
to 3.7e-9 on the reference engine. 251 rows pass, against 246 before.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

1de8057 2026-09-11 The spectral-Poisson floor becomes what the canon already called it
stage0-report.md said the semilocal exchange-correlation ridge extension of
the spectral-Poisson floor was pending. Nothing else was waiting on that
sentence: test-suite.md 9.6 already described the finished construction and
already named it, in those words, the canonical rho-to-V floor every entry
cites, and the flagship member's kill threshold for its charge-density-to-
local-potential task already cited the canonical spectral-Poisson floor by
that exact name. The floor the threshold pointed at did not exist yet.

Semilocal_Xc_Ridge_Features in floors.py stacks density, spectral gradient
magnitude and Laplacian into the ridge's feature columns, reusing
Spectral_Gradient_Magnitude_And_Laplacian rather than differentiating twice.
Poisson_Lines fits a Fit_Standardized_Ridge on these features against the
same mean-removed remainder the climatology already averages, sampling 2000
voxels per training run so no single run dominates the fit, and applies it
pointwise on held-out runs in place of the flat climatology constant. The
pointwise sum of Hartree and the fitted remainder is not already zero-mean
the way Hartree plus the climatology is, so that row alone is scored with
Mean_Removed_Relative_L2 rather than the plain Relative_L2 the other three
rows share.

Measured on the same 42 held-out defect-campaign runs as the other three
rows, Hartree plus the ridge reaches a median of 56.20% mean-removed
relative L2, against 57.26% for Hartree plus climatology. The gain is real
but modest, and that is the expected shape of it: the ridge sees only each
voxel's own local density features and never its grid position, while the
climatology is a positional template averaged over near-identical defect
geometries and already captures most of what position alone predicts. The
existing three rows are untouched, and the existing note explaining why the
raw Hartree term alone exceeds one hundred percent stands as it was.

Three tests land in test_floors.py: two closed-form checks that the new
feature stacking is exactly density, gradient magnitude and Laplacian and
that a standardized ridge over those features recovers a noiseless pointwise
law, and one pool-marked test that repeats the training and scoring loop
independently against the live store and pins the measured improvement.
246 rows pass, against 243 before.

611ddce 2026-09-11 Which devices exist moves behind the seam, and a device word is obeyed rather than accepted
Device selection landed one commit ago in operators/training/, holding the
vendor device names itself and asking whether a card was present by constructing
an engine, attempting a lift, and catching the failure. Both halves are now
where they belong.

The names and the question move to substrate/devices.py, which is on the inside
of the seam and may therefore ask the foreign engine directly instead of
inferring an answer from an exception. That matters beyond tidiness: a lift can
fail for a driver fault, an out-of-memory, a busy card or a malformed device
name, and every one of those was being reported as "this machine has no
accelerator". The caller would then have trained on the host at a fraction of
the speed with nothing raised and nothing logged. Silent downgrade is the
failure this sweep can least afford, because a green suite cannot see it.

operators/training/hardware.py keeps the policy and now spells no device at all:
the house words resolve through Preferred_Device_Name, and asking for an
accelerator on a machine that reports none raises instead of quietly falling
back to the host.

The new rows check the thing the seam cannot: that a device word is obeyed and
not merely accepted. One walks a whole forward pass and collects the device and
width every tensor actually landed on, so the host word is verified to keep the
graph on the host and the preferred word to reach the card at the width it was
asked for. Nothing would otherwise raise about either.

235 rows pass, against 232 before.

d867974 2026-09-11 The loop that stood in for a trainer becomes one, and can be left and returned to
The batch protocol written in Wave 1 and never connected is now what the
loop consumes: batches arrive from Next_Batch out of a generator the seed
owns, so one number replays a whole run, and the round-robin over a handed-in
sequence is gone along with the branch that let a plain array reach the loss
without crossing the lift.

Validation is scored over exchangeable units rather than over points, which
is the rule the rest of the corpus already keeps, and the parameters that
come back are the ones that scored best rather than the ones the last step
left. A patience count stops a run whose score has turned around.

Each validation pass writes the live parameters, the moments, the best so
far and the draw's own state to one archive renamed into place, so a machine
dying mid-session costs the passes since the last one and nothing more.

A step now spends one fused forward where it used to spend a value and a
gradient asked for separately, and the run chooses its card and its width by
house word instead of taking whatever the caller's engine happened to be
pointed at. At three hundred and ninety-two thousand parameters on a batch of
four thousand rows that is 230 ms a step on the host in double and 14 ms on
the card in single.

5c6a7e0 2026-09-11 The wrappers a whole member is built out of can be trained through, on either engine
Residual, Conserving and Conditioned each opened on np.asarray(produced.values,
dtype=np.float64). Under the foreign engine that does not sever the tape
quietly; it raises outright -- "Can't call numpy() on Tensor that requires grad"
-- the moment a lifted weight reaches the wrapper. The three had only ever had
the numpy path, and the house idiom is two: they carried no Forward at all.
residual_correction, whose entire identity is wrappers over a backbone, was
blocked on all three of them.

Each now has the Forward every other part already has, written in dunders and
dispatched substrate operations with nothing coerced on the way through.
Residual adds the correction back with no dtype named anywhere. Conserving takes
its per-channel mean through Mean_Over_Last_Axis and its renormalization ratio
through Sum_Over_Last_Axis, against the quadrature weight the representation
reports. Conditioned reads lifted["condition_scale_weights"] instead of
self.parameter_values[...], which is what puts its two arrays inside the
parameter set an optimizer is handed: they were held outside every one of them
before, so nothing that trains the model could move them. The "one plus the
learned scale" convention is kept, so untrained weights still leave the channels
alone.

Gradients reach through each wrapper to the operator inside it, and to
Conditioned's own weights, and they are the reference engine's own numbers. The
new rows differentiate a squared-error loss on both engines and agree to 9.2e-6
absolute on gradients of size up to 5.6e4 -- 1.6e-10 relative, which is where
central differences at a step of 1e-6 stop. Each row asserts the gradient is not
zero, since a severed tape is exactly a zero that no tolerance would catch. The
one place a zero is correct is stated rather than left to be misread: under
zero_mean a bias that only shifts the field gets exactly 0.0, because the law
takes the shift straight back off again, and lift_weights carries 10.4 in the
same run, so the tape is whole.

The two paths agree at the floor of double precision -- 1.1e-16 for Residual,
5.6e-17 and 1.3e-15 for the two laws, 8.9e-16 for Conditioned.

The exact properties are still exact, and now on both paths. zero_mean leaves
every channel's own mean within 7.6e-17 of zero, measured through an inner
operator whose channels start at visibly different offsets, so a single global
mean could not have passed unnoticed. renormalize_to_electron_count puts the
integrated density 8.9e-16 from the count it was asked for. That tightness is
the point: the archived fields integrate to their own electron_count within
2e-7, so any scale left over at inference is model error, which makes it a free
diagnostic only while it stays exact. The scale Inspect publishes is checked
against the factor actually applied.

last_removed_mean and last_renormalization_scale remain separate named arrays,
and every wrapper still passes its inner operator's state out under an inner.
prefix, now with a row that says so.

226 rows pass, against 218 before.

6cb8e1c 2026-09-10 The kernel at the center of the lineage can be trained, and learns per axis
SpectralKernel opened its forward with np.asarray(..., dtype=np.float64) and
called np.fft.fftn. Under the foreign engine that did not quietly sever the tape;
it raised outright -- "Can't call numpy() on Tensor that requires grad" -- the
moment a lifted weight met np.einsum. The kernel every Fourier member is built
on could not train at all, and honoring lifted[...] for its weights made that
look fine.

It now reaches the engine only through the transform facet, and only through the
real transform. Gradients reach every stored mode weight, and they are the
reference engine's own numbers: the new rows differentiate a squared-error loss
on both engines and agree to 6e-9 absolute on gradients of size up to 39, which
is where central differences at a step of 1e-6 stop. Each row also asserts the
gradient is not zero, since a severed tape is exactly a zero that no tolerance
would catch.

Both dense-oracle rows still hold, and hold far tighter than they had to: the
6-cubed match and the 6-to-8 transfer now differ from the closed-form summation
by 1.4e-16 against a bound of 1e-10.

The mode arithmetic is slicing and joining now, not index arrays, so it stays
differentiable: a whole spectral axis is its tail then its head, and the last
axis stores no negative mode at all -- each is the conjugate of the mode opposite
it, rebuilt on the way in and dropped again on the way out. The kept axes are
centered throughout, running minus kept to plus kept, and a new row walks every
signed mode of a 5x6x7 grid against a whole-spectrum lookup to prove it. A grid
too coarse for its modes is now refused rather than silently aliased.

The manifest advertised "SpectralKernel (factorized)" and the code was the full
per-mode tensor. Separability is now a configuration. The factorized form adds
one per-axis mix instead of storing the product, which is the F-FNO factorization
and never materializes the whole tensor. At the flagship's own budget -- the full
coarse Nyquist of a forty-cubed grid, sixty-four channels each way -- the full
form stores 564,600,832 reals and the separable one 1,007,616. That is 560 times
fewer, and it is what makes twenty modes in three dimensions affordable.

The separable form has its own dense-oracle row, its own transfer row, and its
own gradient row, and Inspect publishes magnitude and phase per axis.

875bbad 2026-09-10 The transform the whole lineage waits on becomes real, and says why it is borrowed
The facet offered the complex pair only. The primitive the suite ranks second in
its union is the batched three-dimensional real transform: rfftn and irfftn, a
little over half the memory and time of the complex pair on a real field.

Both are here now, and both state a contract instead of taking whatever the last
three axes happen to be: at least one leading channel axis, then exactly three
grid axes, checked on the way in and reported by Split_Batch_From_Grid. The
inverse takes the grid shape it is returning to, because a half spectrum cannot
say whether its last axis was even or odd.

Precision is the engine's, and the rule is that a transform never promotes: a
declared width governs, except that an array which arrived narrow stays narrow.
That is the hazard the single-precision engine row already warns about, closed
here rather than left to every caller.

The module docstring records the decision the body rests on. The canon prices an
adjoint wrapper over a vendor transform in days against multi-week for an own
Stockham transform, calling that the suite's largest single kernel. So this is a
wrapper on purpose, with a named successor, and because no member imports a
transform directly, replacing the body later touches no member.

Also here: the engine-neutral pieces a spectral kernel needs to stay behind the
seam -- conjugate, complex-from-parts, reverse, slice, join, zeros, summation,
and the Hermitian mode part.
