02a752a 2026-09-10 The compact-support kernel, in two parametrizations that share one profile
The one part two members were both waiting on. It is two classes, not one, and
the reason is in the code: ContinuousDisplacementKernel evaluates a learned
radial profile at any displacement and fuses as a periodic radius graph, while
TabulatedStencilKernel carries a free weight block at each whole-voxel offset
and fuses as a shift and accumulate around the torus. The parametrization and
the fast algorithm both change with the measure, so one Integrate could not have
served both without forking down the middle.

That they are one kernel family is not asserted, it is tested: Tabulate_On_Grid
writes a continuous profile out onto a grid's own offsets, and the stencil that
comes back reproduces the continuous kernel's integral there to 1e-12, against
the dense reference, at two grid sizes with two different box shapes.

Both kernels meet the dense reference integral on small problems. The continuous
oracle runs on a sheared slab whose third height is a fifth of its lattice
vector, where the cutoff crosses three cells; the reference deliberately
enumerates one ring of images further out than the fused path does, so a reach
taken from vector lengths instead of cell heights fails the oracle rather than
passing it quietly.

Nine tests, each one calibrated by breaking the code it covers. Two rounds of
that found two places where the tests proved nothing: the image sum inside
Tabulate_On_Grid was unreachable under its own no-overlap guard, and the
vanishing of the profile at the cutoff held for the basis alone, envelope or no
envelope. The first is gone and the guard now carries the argument; the second
became an assertion about the flatness of the approach, which the envelope alone
can satisfy.

Inspection lands with the kernels: the stencil as a rank-five field over its box
with its offsets and block sizes beside it, the profile as a curve over the
radius with the basis that built it, and the edge distances of the last forward.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

a34ecca 2026-09-10 The fields stop being re-read: resident in single, and a step's batch drawn from them
Assembling a batch by reopening npz archives costs 5 to 12 ms a step and reads the same
bytes over and over. Held in memory as float32, the whole strain training role is 464 MB
and the perovskite one 217 MB, against 31 GB of corpus, and a step costs 0.6 ms. The cache
holds one CachedField per run -- identifier, path, unit key, parameter vector, values,
grid, lattice, cell volume and covariates -- indexes them by exchangeable unit, reports
what it costs in bytes, and answers Inspect. Its statistics function keeps the values in
single where they live and sums in double where it counts, taking a second pass over the
resident fields for the spread so that a mean far from zero cannot cancel it away.

A batch is a dictionary of named rectangular arrays rather than a fixed record, because
the eleven cards do not agree on what a batch contains: two of the three that exist today
would not fit one shape. The source is a protocol with two questions -- one step's batch,
and the fixed batches a validation pass is scored on, returned as unit key and batch so a
score is a mean over exchangeable units rather than over points. The generator is passed
in and never held, so the trainer owns the seed and one number replays a whole run; the
held-out points are spaced rather than drawn, so no second seed exists anywhere.

Point sampling is what erases the grid spread. The perovskite training role is 199 runs on
100 distinct grids -- one angle stratum all at 64 cubed, and a length stratum whose every
run has a shape of its own -- and eight runs by five hundred and twelve points is one
rectangular array whatever each run's grid was. The pool-marked test takes sixteen runs on
sixteen distinct grids and asserts the rectangle.

The surprise was underneath all of it. The store hands these fields back laid out axis
first, so flattening one to sample it copies the whole field: 262 microseconds per run per
step, seven times the entire cost of assembling a batch, for a gather that takes three.
The cache lays them out in reading order once, at build time. Two tests hold that down --
the resident values are contiguous, and, by indexing the three axes directly and owing
nothing to any flattening, a drawn point's value is what the archive holds in the cell its
coordinate names.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

b86336c 2026-09-10 The census is walked once a process, and a read can ask for the precision the store wrote
Strain_Assignments_By_Run read the whole census and rebuilt the orbit map on every call:
0.748 s measured, paid again for every role and every epoch, to produce a tuple that is
identical every time. It is built behind functools.cache now, keyed on the pool root, and
the warm call costs 0.13 ms. What the cache holds is the tuple the orbit map already
returns, so the shared value cannot be altered; the dictionary each caller receives is
built fresh from it. The tests assert both halves -- the same assignment objects come back
on a second call, and a caller who empties the dictionary they were handed takes nothing
away from the next one.

Field_From_Archive widened every field to double. The store writes charge, magnetization,
localization and potential as float32, so a double read spends twice the memory to hold
numbers that carry no more information than they arrived with. The precision word defaults
to double, which leaves every existing caller where it was, and a caller who wants
residency asks for single. Single to double is exact, so this is a decision about memory
and never about numbers, and the test says so in the only way that settles it: the two
reads are equal after widening, in half the bytes. The word threads down through the
parameter-sweep loaders, because the caller that wants it is the resident cache and it
enters through Parameter_Field_Examples.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

0b7c97e 2026-09-10 The grid's converse: where a handful of chosen points actually are
Fractional_Grid_Coordinates answers where every point of a grid is, in the order the
field values flatten. A point-sampled trainer needs the opposite question -- where are
these five hundred points -- and building sixty-four thousand rows to read five hundred
of them is the wrong way to ask it. Fractional_Coordinates_Of_Flat_Indices unravels in
C order, which is the exact converse of that flattening, and divides by the same extents.

The two agree bit for bit rather than to a tolerance: a whole number divided by its
extent is the same float whether it arrived out of arange or out of unravel_index. The
test asserts exactly that over five shapes, both for every point of each grid and for
sixty-four drawn at random, and checks that an empty draw still answers with a column per
axis. It is a total gate on the one assumption a point-sampled trainer rests on, and it
costs milliseconds, so there is no reason for it to be a spot check.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

ee51bec 2026-09-10 The loss and its gradients come back from one call, because torch already had both
TorchEngine.Gradients materialized the loss, called backward on it, and
threw the loss away. Every caller that wanted the number then asked for
it again, and paid a second forward pass for something it had already
computed. Value_And_Gradients returns the pair, and Gradients is now one
line that takes the second half.

The reference engine has no such saving to offer, so it says so plainly:
evaluate, then differentiate. That is one extra forward on a path that
already spends two per scalar parameter, which is the cheapest thing on
it.

It goes on the Engine protocol, which means both engines had to have it
before either could. A member implemented on one engine only stops that
engine's sibling from structurally satisfying the protocol, and every
`engine: Engine` annotation in the package fails the type gate at once.

Test_The_Fused_Value_And_Gradients_Answers_Both_Separate_Calls runs on
each engine and holds the fused answer against what Evaluate and
Gradients say on their own.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

4a2d724 2026-09-10 Precision stops being a keyword argument and becomes what an engine is
A raw numpy constant reaching a device graph raises, and that is the
kind error one notices. A lifted-but-double constant reaching a single
graph does not raise: float32 + float64 promotes, in silence, and the
graph finishes in double at double the cost with no sign that anything
went wrong. The narrowing therefore cannot be a per-call argument that
one call site forgets. It is a property of the engine, resolved once,
and every lift on that engine resolves its type from it.

Precision is a word now, "single" or "double", with the numpy type it
names beside it. NumpyEngine takes no such argument at all: central
differences at a step of 1e-6 cancel six digits and single precision
has only seven, so the reference engine has no choice to offer. Torch
takes one, defaulting to double, so every conformance row above still
means what it meant.

Both lifts narrow on the host, before transfer, so the bus carries the
graph's precision rather than the master weights'. The master weights
themselves stay double, and so do the optimizer moments: that is the
mixed precision, stated in the docstring so nobody helpfully fixes it.

Test_A_Single_Precision_Engine_Never_Promotes_To_Double follows a
narrowed engine through lifted parameters, a lifted constant, and a
perceptron forward, and then shows the hazard itself by subtracting a
double constant and watching the result come back float64 with nothing
raised.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

bc01311 2026-09-10 The type gate stops asking the shell what it thinks, and two packages enter it
Asking what six parallel agents would see on their first command turned up a suite
that was green only for me. The gate finds its checker with shutil.which, so the
version grading the package was whatever the caller's path offered first -- mine
resolved the environment's checker, a bare shell resolved an older one, and the two
disagreed about a line I had written. Worse than the binary: the checker also infers
which environment's stubs to read from that same path, so it was analysing a different
numpy than the project installs.

Both halves are now pinned. The checker is taken from beside the running interpreter
before the wider path is consulted, and the analysed environment is named in the project
configuration rather than discovered. The suite passes with PATH cut down to /usr/bin,
which is the only version of green that means anything to somebody else.

The line they disagreed about now spells its dtype, which is the lesson this package has
already learned twice from the same stubs.

Two packages I added were never entered in the import gate, so nothing checked that they
import at all. And one test wrote its scratch figures to a fixed directory inside the
repository and removed it afterwards, which two concurrent runs would race on; it uses
the per-test temporary directory the rest of the file already uses.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

2a91d74 2026-09-06 Every component of the member, drawn: thirty-six figures and the gate they had to pass
The result figures join the component ones -- best and worst test prediction against
truth with the signed difference between them, error spread by strain family, and the
member against each floor with the level it had to reach drawn across the bar. Two
functionals, thirty-six figures, 1.3 MB.

Rendering them found two defects in the drawing itself. The difference panel used a
diverging colour map over a range running entirely below zero, so red and blue read as a
sign change on a residual that never changes sign. The mode sheet had the same latent
fault for any field that does not cross zero. Both now choose their map by whether the
range actually spans zero, and the difference panel is forced symmetric about it.

The inspection arrays cache on the corpus volume rather than here, because modes and
means are fields and a field never leaves it. Only the drawings come into the repository,
which is the egress the suite document permits in as many words. Caching them is also
what lets a re-render skip retraining, and re-rendering unchanged arrays is byte-identical
-- so a committed figure moves only when a result does.

And the gate: the doctrine says a member whose specification names no inspection surface
does not launch its build. None of the eight had one, including this member, which I had
already shipped a measured report for. This one now does.

7961b5b 2026-09-06 The renderer that reads only Inspect(), and the seam widens to hold it
One call now draws any part or assembly from its inspection dict alone, dispatching on
rank and name: spectra on a log scale beside their cumulative energy, mode galleries as
contact sheets, weight matrices centred on zero where they span it, and every scalar
sharing one panel because a dot per figure is a page of nothing. On the assembled member
it writes twelve figures and skips nothing.

The seam becomes a directory. It was one file, and mode galleries, mode-magnitude
spectra, attention maps and message-passing graphs across eight members do not fit one
module -- while the alternative, re-exporting the plotting handle into a sibling, is the
loophole the parallel torch seam exists to forbid. A directory is still exactly one named
place. The backend now lives in its own module that every drawing module imports from, so
the file-writing backend is chosen before pyplot loads whichever module is reached first.

Two things the tests found rather than confirmed. Every bias in the package initializes
to exactly zero, so a colour bar over a constant array is degenerate and needed a guard.
And the determinism test failed for a better reason than it was written for: each panel
autoscales to its own structure, so doubling every mode re-rendered byte-identically --
the gallery showed shape and hid magnitude. It now states its range, and a model whose
modes moved no longer commits the same picture.

bf7ff9a 2026-09-06 The inspection surface stops lying about what it holds, in six places
Building a renderer that may read only Inspect() is the completeness test the doctrine
says it is. Before writing one, asking what it would need found six gaps.

Two were the same defect and the dangerous one is the quiet half. A decomposition mode
arrived as a row of a (rank, voxels) matrix and a mean as a bare vector, so a renderer
either fails -- a voxel count does not factor uniquely -- or, worse, draws a perfectly
plausible bar chart of a field and tells nobody. A mode is a field. It is now inspected
with the shape that makes it one, and the readout already held the grid to do it.

The branch encoder published its weights and never its output, which is the one quantity
the whole member exists to produce. The spectral kernel stored a complex weight as two
real arrays and published only the magnitude, leaving half the meaning unreachable. The
trunk features recorded nothing about whether their points were a grid, so a feature
that is a field could not be told from one that is a list. And a single correction key
meant a removed mean under one conservation law and a multiplicative scale under the
other, indistinguishable from the dict -- now two names, and the first zero-dimensional
scalars in the package, which is what the doctrine asked for all along.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

9a775da 2026-09-06 The first member trains and is judged: parameters to charge density, past both its floors
The assembly needed a composition over coefficients, because the template asks for one
over its hidden type and the only one that existed was typed for grids. It holds no
layers, which is what the framework's own documentation says a parameters-to-field map
should do rather than invent an integral to satisfy the template.

The principal-component configuration is first because it needs no point sampling and
no learned trunk, so it exercises splits, basis, floors, metrics, evaluation and report
end to end before any of the expensive machinery exists.

On held-out test orbits it clears both floors: 74.2 and 75.4 percent better than the
ridge against a twenty-five percent bar, 88 percent better than the nearest-neighbor
copy against a factor of two. Four of four comparisons pass.

Two things the report says out loud rather than smoothing over. Orbit weighting moved
the ridge floor from 0.251 to 0.414 percent -- the symmetry-crowded orbits are the easy
ones, and run weighting let them outvote the sparse hard families while halving every
interval. And one block chose the largest budget offered, so that search never settled;
quadrupling it moved the member by a few percent against a margin it clears by seventy,
so the boundary is recorded instead of chased.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

25dc0df 2026-09-06 Results become rows: metrics grouped into what a member reports, and floors into a verdict
The trainer emitted curves and the metrics were loose functions; nothing turned a
trained model, its task card and its floors into the thing a member is judged by.

The aggregation is over exchangeable units, not runs, and that is the whole point.
Symmetry makes many strain runs exact copies of each other -- the test block is 88
runs over 22 orbits -- so scoring runs would let a crowded orbit outvote a lonely one
and would narrow every interval by roughly the square root of four. Each unit
contributes its own median, and the bootstrap resamples units.

A floor comparison carries the suite's own margin and returns a verdict, so a kill is
read off the table rather than argued afterwards. Both margins express as one number:
twenty-five percent better and twice as good are 0.25 and 0.5 of the floor.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

5a28677 2026-09-06 The two floors this member must beat, and the ridge turns out to be the harder one
The suite names two baselines for parameters-to-field members and neither existed.
The ridge from run parameters to basis coefficients had both halves present and never
composed; the nearest-neighbor field copy was nowhere at all. Both land in the shared
floor module, because the manifold decoder maps the same way and will want them
unchanged.

The ridge now standardizes its own parameters. Doing it at the call site was the
existing practice and is a trap: a parameter the sweep never varied has no spread, and
the strain tensor has several.

Measured on the store, and it inverts the suite's expectation. The nearest-neighbor
copy was supposed to be brutal on factorial sweeps; it scores 1.41% while the ridge
scores 0.25%, so the ridge is the binding floor by threefold. The map is close to
linear, which is exactly what makes ridge strong and copying weak. A rank-32 basis
reconstructs the same fields to 0.002%, so the whole error lives in the parameter map
and none of it in the representation.

Those three numbers are now pinned by a test against the live store, which nothing
did before -- the recorded floor values existed only as prose in a committed report.

83f2e57 2026-09-06 The trunk answers on several channels: one shared basis, one branch row each
The readout emitted a single channel with a hardcoded label, so the two-channel
localization variant had nowhere to put its second field. A branch may now arrive as
one latent row per output channel, and the channels share the trunk while keeping
their own coefficients -- the scheme the spec argued for, against the literature's
split-both.

A branch whose row count disagrees with the labels raises rather than broadcasting,
because that shape is a mistake and never an intention. Each channel is checked against
a single-channel readout built on the same seed, so sharing the trunk is proven rather
than assumed.

63687a6 2026-09-06 The trunk stops seaming at the cell face, and gains the axis an energy window needs
A crystal repeats, so x=0 and x=1 name one place. The trunk read the raw fractional
coordinate alongside its waves, and that block is the one that does not wrap: asked the
same point from either face, a fresh trunk answered 0.705102 and 0.625873. The comment
above it claimed periodicity, which held for the waves it described and not for the
block they were joined to.

Coordinate features become a seam. The periodic map puts a constant where the raw
coordinate stood, and the same trunk now disagrees with itself by 4.4e-16 across
equivalent points -- floating dust, fourteen orders down from the seam it replaces,
and guaranteed by construction rather than by training.

The ramped map survives as the ablation and, at one axis, as what the energy trunk
actually wants: energy has no far face to wrap onto, so the raw coordinate is the
right feature there rather than a defect.

The decomposition could project a field onto modes and had a bare function to rebuild

55e24c7 2026-09-03 The energy trunk gets its targets: curves rebuilt from eigenvalues on one aligned window
The rebuild recipe was a bare function with nothing to feed it. Runs now arrive as
parameter vectors beside their state-density curves, smeared at the campaign width,
spin-summed, weight-normalized and carried relative to the valence-band maximum.

The window's ceiling is measured, not chosen: across all 2,680 atlas runs the lowest
top-band minimum sits 8.357 eV above the valence-band maximum, so +8.0 never reaches
a region some run left uncomputed. Nine runs carry 96 bands where the rest carry 8,
and their extra bands begin 17.8 eV up — inside the window the two band sets agree to
the last bit, so identical sampling holds without truncating anything.

The gaps this path walks reproduce the recorded scissor from the other direction:
1.2150 +/- 0.0575 eV over 128 validation pairs against the report's 1.223 +/- 0.057.

The strain and lattice cards named inputs no archive holds, so neither could load

9f0b34a 2026-08-25 The operators package adopts the house style, and its rationale moves to prose
The scaffold was written before the style was stated, so it carried the design
argument inside its docstrings: seventy-nine percent of its non-blank lines
were prose, and most of that prose explained why code existed rather than what
it does. The style forbids that, so the argument moved rather than being
deleted. operators/README.md now holds the anatomy, why the abstract classes
number four, why the measure became data on every representation, why the
integral is fused into each kernel instead of standing alone, and the corpus
facts that constrain implementations. Each operator folder gains an
IMPLEMENTATION.md holding its own mapping, citation, shape, floors, and kill
thresholds.

What remains in the modules states what the code does, in one line each.
Functions take capitals, so a kernel integrates through Integrate and a
composition chains through Apply; the reference integral and the invariance
report take their full names. Parameters that were single letters are named
for what they carry. Two blank lines separate every definition, methods
included. Datatypes needed no change, and module constants keep their capitals.

Renaming the tests would have silently collected nothing, since the collector
looks for a lowercase prefix, so pyproject.toml now names the pattern; the
suite still reports four passed rather than no tests ran.

155112c 2026-08-25 The operators package: the integral-transform anatomy, abstract, and eight empty assemblies
Every neural operator in the suite is one shape - encode, iterate a kernel
integral with a local term and a nonlinearity, read out - so the package is
that shape made literal. Four abstract classes carry the whole vocabulary:
Operator (the root interface, with conditioning in the signature),
Representation (three typed function objects, each carrying its quadrature -
the measure of the integral as data), Kernel (the learned kernel fused with
its integration; five concrete pairings, with the stencil and graph forms
unified as one compact-support kernel over two measures), and Composition
(chaining schemes that own their topology and their backward strategy). The
dense reference integral is declared as the correctness oracle every fused
kernel must match before it is trusted at size.

The eight chosen operators are thin assembly stubs with manifests stating
their mapping in plain words, their citation, their parts, and their suite
card; the deep-equilibrium variant is a configuration of the Fourier
assembly, not a package. Signatures only, no logic: the first build wave is
the designated interface shakedown and may amend these classes with a
recorded reason. Backend deliberately unchosen; arrays are an opaque alias.

Javier reads this document as a computer scientist, not a physicist. Each of

30af6ca 2026-08-20 GPWNO drilled into a provisional entry; ELECTRAFI takes the pending-candidate slot
The eleventh feasibility drill places GPWNO as an IN-LOCAL complement to
DeepDFT with an honest prior that it lands redundant: its published wins are
against DeepDFT baselines far weaker than DeepDFT's own authors obtain, its
public code is not a usable reference on these cells, and its Gaussian
coefficient head needs the expensive equivariant class (a flagged fixed-frame
first build is specified). Kill thresholds against DeepDFT at matched budget
are fixed in advance. The SAD floor is now a measured number (14.8 percent

a1a615f 2026-08-07 Mark the binary artifacts, and record why they are not in LFS
`git clone` fetches LFS objects by default, so with these files committed once and never
revised a clone downloads the same bytes either way. LFS pays when one file is rewritten
many times; `git log --oneline -- '*/results/*'` reports a single commit. Against no
measurable benefit it would add a git-lfs dependency for every cloner, and a clone without
it yields pointer files instead of data — the wrong trade for a public research artifact,
and worse for the reader it is about to be handed to.

The `binary` attributes are the part that earns its place regardless: git will not attempt
a text diff or a three-way merge, and a merged .npz would be a corrupt array that still
looks like a file.

The condition that would change the answer is recorded in the file, with the command that
measures it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

6cd4e0d 2026-08-07 The licensing guard scopes the whole repository, by default rather than by name
It named one tree as a bare string, `feasibility`. Two things were wrong with that. A
renamed tree leaves the guard scanning a path that does not exist — loud, because the
calibration probe writes into the guarded tree and an empty tree is fatal, but a stop
rather than a check. Worse, a library added later is outside the scope by default and the
guard still exits 0. `programs/operator/` was written after the guard and inherited no
protection: 19 MB of artifacts, on a public remote, unscanned.

Scope is now every file git would publish — tracked plus untracked-not-ignored, 417
files against the previous 248 — with exemptions by name and with reasons.

The four benign hits are real and had to be distinguished from content rather than
suppressed. Naming a pseudopotential family is provenance: `[pseudopotential title withheld]` in a
dataset README records which potential produced the numbers, which is what a reader
needs. A header *block* — TITEL, LEXCH, END of PSCTR together — is the file itself. The
three data docs carry the name and none of the other four signatures. The scanner carries
all five because it defines them.

The allowlist matches by **subset**, not by path. An exempt file that acquires a signature
it is not allowed to carry is still a finding, so exempting a path is not a permanent
permission to hold anything. Stale entries are reported and fail the run, because an
exemption whose file has moved is a permission nobody re-justified.

Calibrated, three probes each watched to fail:
  A  POTCAR content in programs/operator/results/ — the tree the old guard could not
     see — caught, exit 1. This is the regression the change exists to close.
  B  an exempt file gaining `END of PSCTR` — caught, exit 1, subset rule firing
  C  a stale allowlist entry — reported, exit 1

  structure OK · 50 pages, 302 owned topics, 62 open questions
  probes: 34 · caught 34 · missed 0 · stale 0
  licensing: 417 files scanned, 4 exempt with reasons, calibration PASS
  pytest: 42 passed

Found while checking what a public push would expose. Nothing had leaked — the answer was
clean both before and after — but it was clean by luck in one tree rather than by check.

bd3dfc8 2026-08-07 Twin trees: the same subpath under the other root reaches the work that realizes it
journals/ was organized by journal/section/page and physics/ by whatever each author

The mirror is section-level and unenforced, because the two sides decompose on
different axes: the operator journal describes anatomy, the operator library describes

only where the twin is real; the rest sits flat. structure/ is created empty against

Six path mechanisms broke, all invisible when correct: three checkers' root walks, the

quantity compared: 248 before, 248 after. And the path-citation sweep is a superset, not
a replacement — narrowing it to the new names stopped it catching legacy forms, and the
calibration caught that as one probe going from caught to missed.

One forced deviation from the approved plan: the plan put programs/ on sys.path, which
would place a directory named `operator` there. `operator` is a path-resolved stdlib
module, not a frozen one, and pandas imports it from frame.py and series.py — so it
would shadow, silently, at import time. Imports go through a programs package instead.

A root conftest.py fixes a fragility that predates this: the suite resolved imports only
under `python -m pytest` from the root, bare `pytest` raised ModuleNotFoundError, and
the contract was written nowhere. Both forms pass now.

7ebb6db 2026-08-02 Elastic constants, and a discrepancy that turns out to be one number
C44 = 570.0 against a published 570.5. C11 - C12 = 932.6 against 932.9.
Both shear combinations land, which is the half that validates the strain
algebra: the deformation gradient's transpose is invisible on every
symmetric strain and wrong on exactly the sheared points that determine
C44, so agreeing there is not free.

C11 and C12 each sit about 5.7 GPa low. The two shifts are equal to within
0.3 GPa, and that equality is the whole story: with dC11 = dC12, C11 - C12
is untouched and the entire discrepancy appears as 3d in C11 + 2C12. Every
shear quantity agrees and only the bulk modulus moves.

So which bulk modulus is right gets settled by a route that shares no code
with this one. A Birch-Murnaghan fit at PBE's own equilibrium, corrected
back to the experimental reference by B0'*dP, predicts 442.1 GPa. This fit
gives 443.6 -- 0.34%. The published 449.3 reconciles to 1.6% against the
same prediction. The residual here is also smaller at every window, 0.072
against 0.129 meV at the recommended one. The difference is not resolved to
a cause and is left visible rather than tuned away.

Two hypotheses were tested and killed. Energy per current volume rather
than reference volume does not tilt the answer, it destroys it: C11 falls
to 306 and C12 goes negative. Their basis as literally written, with no
constant term and the reference energy subtracted, is unstable -- C44 runs
to 697 at the tightest window.

The linear term reproduces its published failure mode. Omitting it costs
15.9% on C44 at |e| <= 0.006 and inflates the residual 70x. That is a
prediction about how the method fails rather than where it lands, and a
96 GPa swing in the right direction does not happen by accident.

One finding for the ledger, recorded as measured: regime #36 is +/-5% on
the elastic tensor, and PBE meets it for C11 (-1.3%) and C44 (-1.4%) and
misses it for C12 (+7.0%). The published fit misses it further at +11.5%,
so this is the functional and not the fit. The test asserts the miss, so if
C12 ever meets the target the finding is flagged as changed rather than
silently passing.

All six sweep families are now read as one series -- 1,179 points -- since
the cubic constants are only jointly determined by normal and shear
strains together.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

f19c8df 2026-08-02 Two routes to the equilibrium volume, and the gap between them is the result
The energy route reproduces the dataset's own published Birch-Murnaghan fit
on all four parameters: V0 11.4133, a0 3.5740, B0 434.2, B0' 3.66, max
residual 0.152 meV against a published 0.15. That fit was made by someone
else, so this is a re-derivation with the answer known in advance rather
than a measurement. A fit landing on 443 would have silently fitted
experiment instead of the data, which is the one failure a self-check
cannot catch.

The stress route is the more interesting half. The oracle never relaxes a
structure -- it says how far a supplied candidate sits from stationarity --
so the equilibrium volume is where the hydrostatic part of that residual
crosses zero. Two independent routes to one number, and they must agree.

They do not, and the disagreement is the point. The gap is Pulay stress, an
incomplete-basis artefact of computing stress at fixed cutoff while the
cell changes: measured here at +0.103%, published at +0.109%. Neither route
can report it alone. It is an Algebraic/MethodEquivalence residual that
grades the calculation's convergence rather than the structure, which is a
thing the oracle can say and a single frame cannot.

The pressure window is calibrated, not chosen. P(V) is convex, so a wide
window biases the root monotonically -- +0.103 at 3 GPa, +0.069 at 10,
and -0.058 at 20, which inverts the sign of a physical effect. The default
sits at 3 GPa where the estimate is flat across 2.0, 2.5 and 3.0. The
calibration is kept executable so a later change to the default cannot
quietly undo it.

The suite also caught a stale constant of my own: eV/A^3 to GPa was written
at the CODATA-2014 elementary charge, 8e-9 relative off the value that has
been exact since the 2019 SI redefinition. Immaterial to a bulk modulus and
wrong regardless.

Frame gains a total energy, which it needed for the energy route and did
not carry. tools/extract_sweep_family.sh makes the fixture reproducible and
pulls only XML -- 60 MB of a 1 GB archive, with nothing licensed unpacked.
Its -C precedes the pattern, because GNU tar applies -C positionally and
the other order silently extracts into the current directory.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

afaaf9a 2026-08-02 The closing run evaluates a quantity the ledger never graded
Building the exit run against real data turned up a hole, and checking the
hole turned up a bigger one.

build-sequence names five quantities the MVP's closing run evaluates. The
ledger's per-observable table carries an accuracy regime for 59 observables
and none of them is the equilibrium geometry. Not the lattice constant, not
the cell volume, not the relaxed structure by any name -- verified against
the observable column alone, with a control that returns six rows for
elastic and modulus, so the search reaches that table. Crystal-structure
prediction is the first of the three capabilities and the ledger never
graded its output.

Worse, no registry row emits an equilibrium geometry either. That is not a
defect: the oracle scores, it does not solve, so the caller supplies a
candidate and the residual says how far its stress is from vanishing. The
lattice constant is a readout of where that residual crosses zero across a
volume series. But no row names the readout, so the status cell says
anchor-met, path-open rather than path-met.

The number that would otherwise have been used as the gate is a
measurement's uncertainty, and it does not survive contact with a
functional. Against the battery's +/-0.001 A, the diamond sweep's own
verified fits sit 7x out at PBE and 20x out at the gap-calibrated hybrid --
both of them correct physics. So the regime is +/-1%, stated in the
computed currency, and whichever functional produced a0 must be named
beside it, because the two functionals differ by more than the regime is
wide.

The practice journal already warns that a declared error target is not a
measured error. This is that confusion running the other way: a
measurement's uncertainty is not a target either. It says how well the
world was observed, not how well a method must reproduce it. Third row of
this class after delta_meta, which is what makes it a class.

Max phonon energy is also named in the closing run and also absent from the
headline table, but regime #9 already covers it at +/-5% optical, and PBE's
zone-centre mode sits at -1.9%. Added for completeness, not as a defect --
an earlier draft of this finding called it a hole and was wrong.

561f9a7 2026-08-02 Test the checks in both directions, and find a floor that is not zero
Seventeen tests where there were none. Every invariant is now exercised
twice: silent on a well-formed diamond frame, and firing on a frame
corrupted in exactly the way that check exists to catch. A check that only
demonstrates it fires cannot tell you it fires too often, which is why
check_the_checker.py carries negative probes and why these do too.

The first version of the corruption test was wrong, and the test caught it
rather than me. Corrupting n_electrons fired two checks, because the
electron count is legitimately read by the parity check as well. Shared
channels are now declared as exact sets, so a corruption leaking into an
undeclared check still fails.

Two checks added. occupations-within-unit-interval is Pauli, and it fires
on a spin-degenerate occupancy of 2 recorded where the schema expects 1 --
a parser bug that otherwise produces plausible numbers.
kpoint-weights-sum-to-one guards every k-integrated quantity at once,
because unnormalised weights make the electron count, the density of states
and every transport integral wrong by the same silent factor.

That second one does not read zero on real output, and the reason is worth
recording. It reads 6.5e-7. The weights are exact rationals over a 7x7x7
mesh -- 172 irreducible points -- and their exact sum is 1, but VASP prints
them to eight decimals and the truncation accumulates. So this residual has
a noise floor set by the producing file's print precision rather than by
the calculation, roughly 4e-9 per k-point, and it is the only one of the
seven that does. Any tolerance placed on it must sit above that floor or it
fires forever on correct data. Measured, then documented in the check.

The real-data tests skip when /Pool is absent, and nothing from it is
committed: every run directory carries a licensed pseudopotential and this
remote is public.

e58d1fe 2026-07-31 Run the angle predicted to find nothing, and catch our own error with it
Pass B angle 3 tested the corpus's claim that its seven state slots are
the irreducible degrees of freedom of the micro tier. Two of the four
results are killed premises -- nuclear quantum effects survive the
literature test, and the emergence argument for non-equilibrium
distributions is defended with its failure regime refused. The closure
claim fails twice.

The vector potential has no conjugate momentum. Every other dynamical
slot arrives as a canonical pair with its own symplectic block and its
own residual category; A arrives alone, while the corpus asserts its
Maxwell block is canonical, which is a claim about a pair. In the Coulomb
gauge the corpus fixes, the missing partner is the transverse electric
field, and it is independent initial data rather than anything recoverable
from A. The energy functional is written as a functional of A and its
integrand contains E-perp. So the functional derivative has no computable
value on that block and the EOM/A residual has no right-hand side.

The state also cannot tell carbon-12 from carbon-13. Species is atomic
number over an elemental vocabulary; the ion mass occurs exactly once in
the corpus, in the kinetic energy. This is a diamond-first build whose
flagship material has the largest known isotope effect, and whose own
traps page enforces an isotope declaration on every thermal-conductivity
reference row that the state type cannot carry.

This corrects our own pass C recommendation, which called the vector
potential a one-word fix. It is not: deleting "external" leaves the
equation of motion undefined. The state type is now incomplete in three
independent ways found by three separate routes, and should be reopened
once rather than patched three times.

43 of 43 new quotations grounded. Both gates green. Corpus untouched.

6ffcda8 2026-07-31 No wording of a path is true across a move, so stop writing paths
A builder asked whether the reference data moves to data/ at cutover, and pointed out
that the answer does not help: the contract says pages state what is true in the
present tense, and a path reference is false either before the move or after it.
Writing tomorrow's path makes the corpus briefly wrong about itself; writing today's
makes it wrong afterwards. Present tense cannot disambiguate a claim about a location
that is about to change.

The corpus already had the answer and was not applying it to this one class. The
reason the pages could be moved wholesale without breaking a reference is that nothing
cites a path — everything cites an id. Data files were the sole exception, and the
cutover is exactly the move that would have broken them. The contract has said "never
cite by file path, paths move" all along, and the corpus was violating it seven times
in five pages.

Data artifacts now carry ids, declared in the contract and emitted into the index:
registry, reference-data, strain-sweep, research-log, corpus-index. They are cited
like pages, carry no anchors, and need no dependency edge because there is no page on
the other end. All seven references converted; nothing under journals/ names a path.
Moving a file at cutover is now one edit to a map.

The builder also declined to write content I routed to it, on the grounds that its own

know which page owned the area, rather than naming one it had not verified. That was
my omission to answer, not its to guess.

Twenty-nine probes, twenty-nine caught, no error site unreached.

The complexity argument now sits on purpose-and-scope: finding a ground state is
NP-hard, the local Hamiltonian problem is QMA-complete so quantum hardware does not
rescue it, and checking a candidate is cheap — which is why the system is built
around the check and why score-not-solve is a consequence rather than a preference.

The lattice-transpose trap is in the register: matrices are stored by row, so every
transpose is a chance to be wrong and stay plausible, and the metric-tensor form
removes the hazard instead of warning about it. A software defect, not a physics one,
which is why a physicist reading the equations would not catch it.

The invariant battery is on the reference battery: closed-form checks a correct run
already satisfies, which catch parser bugs and run failures together — both otherwise
invisible, since the run exits cleanly and the numbers look reasonable.

Futamura's first projection names what compose-time does. The synthesis-loop framing
and the reason Grover does not apply are on the training stages.

reached. Only one of those could be true. Emitting is now defensive about input the
checker has already reported on, because a checker that dies on bad input is a
checker that reports nothing while returning a failure someone will read as the
finding itself.

2b0c3ad 2026-07-31 The rename was not enforced in the form the tags are actually written
The vocabulary check ran against prose, and prose strips inline code. Tags live in
inline code — the old convention put every short symbol in backticks precisely to
hold the differentiability tag apart from the wurtzite deformation potential of the
same spelling. So the check was reading the one place tags never appear. Verified by
probe: D2, T3 and B7, backticked, all passed silently. The rename had no teeth.

The rule is now the inverse and is stricter for being narrower. A retired tag is a
code span whose entire content is the token. A backticked D2 is caught. C2/m is a
space group and passes. An unbacked D1 is a deformation potential and passes. The old
corpus built the blunt version of this check, measured it against real prose, and
deleted it for crying wolf; the precise version only became available once the
corpus's own names stopped colliding with the physics.

The calibration gained a negative probe, which it should have had from the start. It
now asserts both that the rule fires on a backticked tag and that it stays silent on
an unbacked one. A check that only proves it fires cannot tell you it fires too
often, and over-firing is what got the previous attempt deleted.

Three pages are exempt, each with its reason printed on every run. They are the three
a reader consults about words, which is why they are the three that must quote them.
One of them turned out to hold the argument for this whole decision, as a standing
trap: any short alphanumeric token the corpus invents will collide with a physical
quantity somewhere, and no checker can separate the two by shape.

Also restores two facts that the dissolution of the cross-cutting page dropped, found
by the builder that dissolved it grepping the tree afterward rather than assuming.

overpredicts diamond conductivity by 31% at 1000 K — and the corpus records the
2026-06-10 re-audit as having missed a conductivity overprediction and a mis-citation.
Those are very likely these.

That paper also carries a trap worth registering. Its quoted "2200 to 1400 at room
temperature" figures are boron arsenide, not diamond, and collide numerically with
diamond's own 2200 one paragraph away. Anyone re-deriving the anchor can land on the
right number for the wrong material and see nothing wrong.

Two rows have no source and should say so. The static permittivity feeds image-force
lowering; the Debye temperature sets the four-phonon window the 773 K row depends on,
and carries plus or minus 50 K against a literature spread of 1860 to 2230 by method —
an uncertainty narrower than the disagreement between methods.

Leads, not verdicts. Whether any value is right is the next auditor's question.

85da768 2026-07-31 Give the checker a partial mode, and catch it losing sight of itself
The page graph is cyclic by design — two pages each explain the other — so no order
exists in which every citation resolves as it is written. Without a partial mode an
agent writing one page sees failures belonging to pages nobody has written yet, and
the tempting fix is to edit the page that is not at fault. That is how four correct
registry rows were retagged once already.

--partial downgrades only "cites a page that does not exist yet", keeps every other
check hard, and prints what it downgraded. A partial run that read like a clean one
would be worse than no run.

The calibration caught two things about itself while this landed. Its fixture used to
be a copy of the live corpus, so it failed whenever a page was mid-write — pressure
to weaken the calibration rather than fix the page. It now builds a minimal fixture
of its own. And routing two findings through a local alias dropped them out of the
coverage scan silently: twenty-three sites became twenty-one with nothing reported.
The scan now follows every sink, not one name. That drop is the failure this script
exists to catch, one level up, and it only surfaced because the count is printed on
every run.

Twenty-three probes, twenty-three caught, no site unreached.

Also lands the first real page. It states its own missing wire schema under a heading
rather than leaving the seam's forward reference to resolve to silence.

dbd0c2a 2026-07-31 Build the enforcement before writing a single page
Phase 2a. The contract, the checker, and the proof that the checker looks.

The rules are not written in the checker. They are parsed from the last yaml block
of the agent contract, so the document an agent reads and the enforcement it faces
are one artifact and cannot drift apart. Changing a rule means changing that block.

Frontmatter is smaller than the old corpus's. Journal and section are read off the
path, because a field that restates the path can disagree with it — the lesson the
old tree already learned when it deleted chapter, chapter-name and tag. Nothing in a
page is restamped by a tool: no content hash, and referenced-by is emitted into
corpus.json rather than written back into frontmatter.

Citations carry a declared anchor. Section ordinals are gone, and not for the reason
first given — they do not merely rot, they were never checked into 33 of 58 pages,
because the old resolver skipped any target with no numbered headings. A citation to
a section that did not exist passed.

Twenty-three probes, twenty-three caught, none missed, none stale, and no error site
in the checker that no probe reaches. That last number is derived from the checker's
own syntax tree rather than from a list beside it, so the coverage claim cannot drift
from the code it describes. This project has shipped the opposite: a "one defect per
check" claim that was false of thirteen classes while a clean run was cited as
evidence for them.

One page is exempt from the history-marker sweep, because a page that forbids a word
has to be allowed to name it. Every run prints the exemption. A clean run that hides
its own holes measures the checker, not the corpus.

Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Hk2A5ZpEL4ob6zJ1msmQZX

6d83b6a 2026-07-31 Recover the vocabulary the corpus never wrote down
Phase 1.5. Every corpus-invented tag family, named in English.

S1-S5 fills the Source column on all 132 registry rows and canon defines it
nowhere; the only gloss sits on a page whose other legend is known-wrong. Recovered
twice instead — from usage, where Source cells cite "defects G.1" and "non-eq H.1"
against section letters those pages actually use, and from the commit that migrated
seven research streams. The two agree on the rows they overlap, which is what
licenses trusting either.

T0-T3 was reported as three incompatible legends, and calling the registry canonical
would have deleted a live classification. The registry classifies how expensive one
evaluation is; the operator classifies how often the training loop calls it. Neither
is stale, they belong to different libraries, and purpose-and-scope forbids the
oracle from owning loop policy. They already mis-bind: an SCF residual is T3 by cost
and T2 by cadence, off by one on exactly the five most expensive rows. Third instance
of one pattern, after coverage-mask and source — a token shared across the seam with
a different meaning on each side.

Every one already carries its English name in the same table as the serial. The
serials were decoration; what they added was the collision that retagged four correct
rows once already, when a checker read L1-the-level as L1-the-bundle-value.

Also registers what the seeded values rest on. Of 179 reference-data rows, 24 carry
no author and no year. Five are declared gaps working as intended. The rest point at
this corpus rather than at literature, or state a method where a source belongs. Two
diamond conductivity rows cite the re-audit the corpus records as having missed a
conductivity overprediction.

Phases 0 and 1 of the scaffolding-free restructure. Eleven read-only surveyors

but deliberately unresolved — that register is the next auditor's starting corpus,

The corpus itself is untouched and both checkers are still green.

What the sweep found that planning had not:

- 47% of page-id citations use a syntax no checker reads. One page is 91%
  unchecked, and it is the page claiming the checkers hold it to the same rules
  as every other.

  target with no numbered headings, so a citation to a section that does not
  exist passes.

2af93d2 2026-07-22 Check table shape and band interiors; write down the three checks that cannot exist
**Table shape.** One unescaped `|` turns a cell into three and the table
renders wrong while every by-name check keeps passing — the same failure the
generated `index.md` shipped. 20 rows were broken: 17 carried absolute-value
bars inside code or math spans (`μ₀|E|/v_sat`, `O(|G|/|H| · rank)`,
`$a = |\mathbf a_1|$`), which GFM splits on even inside a span; 3 were alloy
rows in a binary-endpoint table carrying one spanning value where the header
wants two. The first kind is escaped, the second given the `—` placeholder the
table already uses for exactly that case.

**Band interiors.** `rows 113–119` asserts a contiguous run exists, and only
the endpoints were checked — a hole at 116 read as fine. Now the interior is
checked too. Zero violations today; it is a regression guard.

Band *coherence* is deliberately not enforced, and the measurement is why.
Bands are two different things. `rows 113–119` is a semantic package and all
seven share `B6`; `rows 1–102` and `rows 88–102` are structural claims about
the registry's layout and share nothing by design. Requiring a common bundle
would have fired on seven correct bands. Measured before writing, this time.

**And a section recording what this tool will not check.** Three checks were
written, run, and removed:

  - tolerance-ledger exhaustiveness — `τ` is not a reserved prefix; 48 hits on
    correct prose (`[traps]` §69)
  - bare `§` self-references — `Öttinger 2005 §5.3` is shaped identically to a
    corpus coordinate
  - legend scoping — `D0`-as-charge-state versus `D0`-as-diff-tag are the same
    token, and a `D[+-]` pattern also matches the word "D-tag"

Each was built on an invariant that a page asserted and the corpus did not
honour. They are listed in the tool's header so the next person does not
rebuild them and read the noise as signal — and because the boundary of what
green covers should be legible from the thing that prints it, which is the
complaint this whole pass started from.

287d2eb 2026-07-22 Restore rows 91-94: L1 was never a defect, and my "fix" corrupted them
The previous commit reported registry rows 91-94 as carrying a Born-Oppenheimer
level in the Bundle column and retagged all four to `B1`. That was wrong. `L1`
is a documented, deliberate value, and two canon pages say so:

  named-formulas (which owns the schema for this field):
      bundle : {BundleId}  -- one or more of B1..B11, or the L1 primitive tag
                           --   (linear-response primitives Z*/ε∞/χ∞/α_M,
                           --   rows 91-94, feed multiple bundles)

  canonical-vocabularies, in the paragraph directly beneath its own table:
      "The four linear-response primitive rows 91-94 ... carry the `L1` tag
       instead of a B-bundle: they are level-1 primitives feeding multiple
       bundles."

The check harvested its vocabulary from the bundle *table* and never read the
page that owns the *field*. It then reported four correct rows as defects, and
I edited the registry to match the checker rather than reading the two pages
that would have stopped me. The rows are restored to `L1`.

This is worse than the false positive in `[traps]` §69. That one fired on
correct prose and was caught before anything was edited. This one silently
corrupted the data the check existed to protect, and it survived a commit
message that described the retag as well-evidenced. The evidence I cited — that
`B1`'s BO level is `L1`, so the slip was "legible" — was a story that fit the
symptom. `[traps]` §61 already says a dangling pointer is safer than a plausible
reconstruction; this was a plausible reconstruction with write access.

Landed as `[traps]` §70: harvest a vocabulary from the schema that owns the
field, not from the enumeration sitting next to it — and when a check fires on
data that has been stable for months, suspect the check first.

The check now unions both sources and passes on `L1`. Its probe no longer
plants `L1` either: planting it would have asserted the false positive as
correct behaviour and frozen the bug into the calibration. It plants `L4` — a
real BO level that is genuinely not an admitted bundle value — so the class of
slip is still covered.

74189be 2026-07-22 Check the two registry axes nobody was checking, and the human spelling of a retired name
Three new check families, one abandoned, and a repair to the previous commit.

**The unchecked axes.** `Diff` got a checker after a Diff incident and `Tier`
after a Tier incident; `Bundle` and `Path` appeared in neither tool. A defect
had been sitting in `Bundle` the whole time — registry rows 91-94 read `L1`,
which is a Born-Oppenheimer level, not an observable bundle. The canon table
lists bundles as `| B1 | electronic-structure | L1 |`, code then name then BO
level, and whoever entered those rows took the third column.

Retagged to `B1`. Rows 92-94 are unambiguous: they take `γ̂` and return Z*, ε∞,
χ∞ — electronic response, and `B1`'s BO level is exactly the `L1` that was
miscopied, so the slip is legible. Row 91 (`lattice-coulomb-summation-scalar`,
the Madelung constant) is the one I am inferring: its signature is
`(lattice) → α_M` with no electrons in it, and it could argue for `B4`
alongside the charged-supercell corrections. It is in `B1` because all four
rows were entered together carrying the identical wrong value. **Worth a
second opinion.**

Every coded column is now validated against a closed vocabulary, with the
bundle codes harvested from the page that owns them rather than restated here.

**The human spelling.** `retired-names.csv` catches `Born-stability-cubic`;
prose writes "Born stability", and for months only the first was swept. Also
"Coffin–Manson" with an en-dash, "Schottky–Mott", "Hume-Rothery", "Pauling
radius ratio". The prose forms are derived from the identifiers — a
`prose-form` column on 62 rows would be 62 more things to keep in sync — and
25 unresolved uses were found and resolved in place.

Auto-inserting "(now `X`)" got five of them wrong, including one that rewrote
the inside of a quoted phrase and changed what the sentence was quoting. Those
five are hand-corrected. Mechanical prose edits need reading afterwards.

**The tolerance ledger** is now derived from the page that owns it: 17 symbols
instead of 9 hard-coded here. The old comment recorded the 53% coverage
honestly and nothing acted on it for months.

**One check written and then abandoned**, recorded as `[traps]` §69. The
ledger states an exhaustiveness rule — "a tolerance stated anywhere but absent
from this table is a defect in this table" — so I made it executable. It fired
48 times on correct prose: `τ_n` and `τ_p` are SRH carrier lifetimes, `τ_PO` a
scattering time, `τ_E` an energy relaxation time. The checker was correct with
respect to its premise; the premise — that this page's claim "`δ`/`τ` denotes
a *tolerance* throughout" describes the corpus — was false. The overclaim is
deleted, the rule is labelled a review rule, and the trap says why: a checker
inherits the soundness of the invariant it rests on, and one that cries wolf
teaches the reader to skim, which is the disease.

The same reasoning killed a bare-`§` self-reference check: `Öttinger 2005
§5.3`, `Jackson 1998 §17.2` and `Blondel et al. §3.4` are literature
citations, and nothing mechanical separates them from corpus coordinates.

**Repair to the id rename.** That migration required an id token before a `§`,
so it never touched bare self-references — `2.3-gamma-hat.md` was left citing
`§15.2` against its own renumbered sections. 81 of those, plus 3 trailing
coordinates in multi-coordinate citations (`id §a, §b` — only §a was rewritten)
and 5 stale cross-page coordinates. The checker could not see any of them,
which is why they shipped.

**Performance.** `check_data_agreement.py` ran in 3.0 s and calibration runs it
once per probe — 55 of them — which tripped a two-minute timeout. The cost was
62 retired names each wrapped in its own `(?<![\w-])...(?![\w-])`. Hoisting the
lookarounds out of the alternation: 2.53 s -> 0.14 s, identical hits. Tool now
runs in 0.30 s, full calibration in 18 s.

2f1d22f 2026-07-22 Give every page a name instead of a serial
Thirty-eight of 58 page ids carried a `<tree>-NN-` prefix inherited from the
pre-book tree, and the number in it agreed with nothing:

    file:  4.3-representations.md          says 4.3
    id:    arch-20-representations         says 20
    cite:  arch-20 §20.3   (x46)           says 20

One page, three numbers. The other 20 pages already had bare descriptive ids
— `traps`, `timeline`, `accuracy-ledger`, the nine `deriv-*` — so this
finishes a pattern the corpus had already started rather than imposing a new
one.

The surface was larger than the plan claimed. I said ~319 `[id]` references;
the real figure is 384, of which only 109 are brackets. The other 275 are
short-form citations (`arch-20 §20.3`) that carry the serial *inside the
citation*, and 15 pages number their sections after the id, so ids and section
coordinates could not be separated. Renaming ids alone would have produced
`representation-substrate §20.3` — a name with no 20 in it pointing at a
section numbered 20, which is worse than what it replaced.

So both moved together. Sections are now bare ordinals under the id:

    representation-substrate §3

Deliberately NOT numbered after the display tag (`§4.3.3`), which the plan's
preview showed. The conventions call the tag presentational and regenerate it
freely; numbering sections after it would make the disposable thing
load-bearing and break a page's own 46 citations the first time it changed
chapters. The id is the contract, so the coordinate hangs off the id.

Two pages needed more than prefix-stripping. `cert-obligations` numbered
12.0.1 / 12.0.2 / 12.0.3 / 12.1, which strips to §0.1 .. §1; `multiscale-state`
opens at 21.0, which strips to a bare §0. Both are regrouped so the top-level
group renumbers from 1 while depth survives — 21.2.1 stays a child of its
parent. A first attempt renumbered every coordinate in document order and
turned 21.2.1 into 4, a sibling of 3, silently flattening the hierarchy. Caught
in dry-run.

Also landed:
  - 28 files renamed so the slug matches the id (`git mv`, history preserved)
  - `retired-ids.csv`, mirroring `retired-names.csv`, mapping all 38 old ids —
    this is what keeps the frozen records under `live/audits/` readable
    without editing them
  - the short-form prefix resolver in `apparatus.py` deleted as dead code:
    no id matches `(arch|impl|mvp)-\d+-` any more
  - the section-coordinate harvester fixed. It required a dot
    (`\d+(?:\.\d+)+`), built for the old `20.1` scheme, so it harvested no bare
    ordinals at all and 160 citations resolved to nothing. It now reads the
    leading number of a heading and only that — matching every number in the
    line would harvest "the 12 computational methods" as a coordinate.
  - `calibrate.py` no longer crashes when a probe's target file is missing.
    Renaming 28 pages made every path in it wrong at once and it raised
    FileNotFoundError mid-run, reporting nothing about any other probe. A
    missing target is now STALE — which is what this file says a moved corpus
    should produce.

The unresolvable-reference check caught this commit's own prose: the
conventions paragraph explaining why ids should not be bare common nouns wrote
`[state]` as its example, and the checker flagged it. Reworded, and the
incident recorded there.

Not swept: `informed-operator/design/training-stages.md` carries 3 old ids. It
is untracked and being written by a concurrent session in this worktree;
editing it would silently clobber work in flight. It needs a pass once that
session lands.

7fb6e63 2026-07-22 Delete the frontmatter that said nothing, derive the rest
Measured across all 58 pages: 613 of 1,517 frontmatter lines — 40% — were
hand-maintained and carried no information.

  status          58/58 read `draft`. This page spent seven lines justifying
                  keeping a field it admits "carries no information: do not
                  read it as a signal." A field shaped like a signal that
                  carries none is worse than no field.
  tag             100% redundant with the filename. Checked every page: zero
                  mismatches, because it cannot be anything else.
  chapter         redundant with the containing folder, and there was a
  chapter-name    checker whose entire job was confirming they agreed.
  research-sources  non-empty on none, still mandatory in the template.
  revision        documented with rules for when to bump it, on a field that
                  existed on zero pages.

`chapter`, `chapter-name` and `tag` are now read off the path
(`apparatus.shelf_position`). Moving a page between chapters is a `git mv` and
nothing else. The chapter-versus-folder check is deleted with the redundancy
that needed it: an invariant held by construction beats one held by a checker.

`referenced-by` is now generated. It was 381 hand-kept entries mirroring 381
`depends-on` entries exactly, guarded by a two-direction symmetry check that
existed solely to police the duplication. It is derived and restamped like
`content-hash`; asymmetry and dangling reverse entries are no longer
detectable-but-possible, they are unrepresentable. Faithfulness was proved
before the migration: the generator reproduced all 381 hand-written entries
with zero diff.

Calibration then did its job. Five probes stopped testing anything — two
STALE against deleted fields, three MISSED because the checks they targeted
had become better checks. The two are deleted; the three now plant the three
ways a derived field can be wrong (an entry too many, an entry naming nothing,
an entry missing after a new forward edge) and all expect staleness.

And the probe count is now derived rather than transcribed. It moved 50 -> 52
-> 50 during this commit alone, touching five files each time — the same churn
the complaint sheet recorded as 45 -> 47 -> 48 -> 49 -> 50 over one pass.
`apparatus.py` restamps it in prose the way it restamps a hash, so nobody
types it again. The check behind it now reads `README.md` and
`instructions.md` too: it only ever walked `pages/`, so two of the five sites
quoting the number drifted unwatched, one of them the file an agent reads
first.

Not done, contrary to plan: collapsing the seven-level authority order. The
plan asserted levels 6 (`journal/live/`) and 7 (`README.md`) "are never
consulted in any tie the corpus has actually had." That was my claim, not a
verified one — and `live/specs/` losing to canon is a distinction this corpus
uses. Removing information on an unverified plausible claim is the failure
mode this base exists to prevent, so the order stands until someone checks.

9044eec 2026-07-22 Retire the "proposer" from the corpus; the operator is the only learner
Main carried uncommitted edits removing every "proposer" reference from the
two purpose-and-product pages and the code-spec brief. The corpus described a
"learned proposer" as though it were a specified component. It is not: no
proposer is specified anywhere in the corpus, and inverse design is explicitly
out of scope for /physics (arch-17-out-of-scope). What exists is the neural
operator, whose training loss the oracle's keyed residuals and cotangents
enter.

  1.2-product    discrete-variable search is "an external search over that
                 discrete space", not "an external proposer"
  1.5-rationale  the grader disciplines "the learned operator"; the operator,
                 not a proposer, supplies the complete candidate state
  the brief      states the oracle's role as the physics-informed residual-loss
                 term for training the operator, and demotes property-targeted
                 design from an assumed mechanism to a stated direction

The brief also gained two distinctions it was missing. Refusal has two regimes
that must not be conflated: a compose-time degeneracy is refused there with a
numeric witness, while at runtime there are no exceptions at all and failure
surfaces as a Failed cert leaf carrying its witness — separate surfaces,
separate enumerations. And the oracle is numerics-agnostic at the seam but
committed to its representation substrate within; the boundary is portable,
the interior is not.

13f9afa 2026-07-22 Fleet round 5: every citation verified against primary sources — one misattributed, one cherry-picked, one unattributed
The citations auditor checked all eight external works. Metadata is exact
everywhere: no fabricated work, no wrong volume, issue, year or page range
anywhere in the corpus. Three claims still had to change, and two of them are
mine from c0e345f, where I said I had checked against primary sources.

MISATTRIBUTION. "Its error bounds are independent of small singular values
(Kieri, Lubich & Walach, SIAM J. Numer. Anal. 54, 2016)" -- where "Its" is the
BUG integrator. KLW 2016 predates BUG by six years and says nothing about it; it
proves the property for PROJECTOR-SPLITTING. The corpus's own other citation
refutes the attribution: Ceruti & Lubich, BIT 62(1) 23-44 (2022) says BUG "retains
the important robustness to small singular values that has so far been known only
for the projector-splitting integrator." The physics was right -- BUG genuinely is
robust -- only the pointer was wrong. Corrected at 10.2 and at 2.3, where KLW had
been stretched to cover "basis-update & Galerkin" too.

THE 104 WITHOUT THE 135, and this is the one that matters. Three sites cited
egglog as "faster, and more accurate in 104 of 289 benchmarks", concluding
"soundness is not a tax; it paid". The paper's VERY NEXT SENTENCE reports that the
unsound rule set won on accuracy in 135 cases. On accuracy the sound version LOST
MORE OFTEN THAN IT WON. Nothing in the quoted half was false, every number checked
out against the source, and the conclusion drawn from it was not the paper's. What
the paper shows is that soundness is AFFORDABLE -- free in time, roughly even in
accuracy, and it solved one benchmark the unsound set could not solve at all.
Fixed at all three sites and recorded as trap 68: when a result is a comparison,
quote both arms and read the sentence after the one you are lifting.

UNATTRIBUTED. The NP-hardness of general-DAG checkpoint scheduling is not
Griewank & Walther's -- it is Naumann, "DAG reversal is NP-complete", J. Discrete
Algorithms 7(4) 402-410 (2009). At 4.4 it sat INSIDE the revolve parenthetical,
reading as one source for both. Now cited at all three sites, with revolve's own
reference completed (ACM TOMS 26(1) 19-45, 2000). Also flagged that the Herbie
"deleting the rules made it useless" fact is Zhang et al.'s finding ABOUT Herbie,
not a claim in Herbie's own PLDI 2015 paper.

Blondel 2022 RESTORED and promoted. I had pulled it from the glossary pending
verification; it checks out as "Efficient and Modular Implicit Differentiation"
(NeurIPS 35, 5230-5242) and does support the claim. It now sits on the OWNING page
-- 4.2's adjoint-synthesis bullet, which stated the claim bare -- with the caveat
the auditor supplied: "one linear solve" means one SYSTEM, itself solved
iteratively, not O(1) work.

Softened 5.1's tau_trunc row, which said computable estimates exist "for exactly
this quantity". Ehrhardt & Roberts assume a strongly-convex lower-level
OPTIMIZATION; an SCF inner solve is a general nonlinear fixed point, so the
transfer is by analogy. Blondel §3.4 is closer in setting but bounds the error in
terms of the unknown x*, so it is not a-posteriori computable -- both now cited,
each for what it actually gives.

And inserting trap 68 put it ABOVE 67, which left no gap and no duplicate, so the
trap check passed a register reading 66, 68, 67. Order is now checked too.

apparatus OK. seams clean. calibration 50/50, coverage complete across 16 classes.

c55e969 2026-07-22 Fleet round 3: a note calling an identity an inversion, a retired rule still doing work, and "uniformly" refuted three paragraphs above
11.7 row 31 gave the Cowley-Sze barrier two ways and then said "The
S(phi_M - phi_CNL) form INVERTS it" -- one clause after calling them equivalent.
They are algebraically identical: expanding phi_M - chi - (1-S)(phi_M - phi_CNL)
gives phi_CNL + S(phi_M - phi_CNL) - chi exactly, and both limits are right
(S=1 Schottky-Mott, S=0 Bardeen). Verified on 1000 random points. The note is
residue of a REAL pinning-parameter inversion fixed elsewhere in an earlier pass,
over-applied to a rearrangement that inverts nothing -- and the row's own residual
then uses the "inverting" form, so the note contradicted the line under it.

11.7 Part E justified AlN's QHA breakdown with "773 K > Theta_D/2". That is the
rule [traps] §40 exists to forbid: it was back-fitted from diamond alone and
contradicts its own GaN example (Theta_D/2 for GaN is 27 C, not 500 C). Row 14 of
the same file had been swept to the per-material statement; this row was missed.
The three failure points sit at 0.49, 1.29 and 0.77 of Theta_D -- no fraction fits.

11.5 Part H closed with "UWBG radiation tolerance is uniformly better than Si by
~1-2 orders of magnitude ... diamond is the best (E_d ~ 45 eV vs Si's ~21 eV)".
Both halves are refuted by §H.1 three paragraphs above, which lists GaN at ~20 eV
-- at or BELOW Si -- so not uniform; and 45/21 is 2.1x, not 1-2 orders of
magnitude, a number the same sentence supplies.

6.7 listed B10 and B11 contents under eponyms retired on 2026-07-21 -- "Pauling

V2". In V1 it returns a constant: no fixed point, no fixed-point Jacobian, so the
D3 conditioning guard and the vJp/JvP gate would both pass it trivially. That is
§7.5's spurious-pass failure one level worse -- the gradient is not locally zero,
it is structurally absent. The gate is now DEFERRED for dormant rows and must be
recorded as deferred rather than emitted as a pass; the rule generalizes to any
row whose source declares it dormant or anchored.

e6b1946 2026-07-22 Finish the epsilon design: four items I claimed in the last commit and had not done
Asked whether that part was finished, I checked against the plan's own
verification section instead of answering from memory. It was not. Four items,
and three share one shape: THE DESIGN WAS STATED AND THE THING THAT WOULD HOLD IT
WAS NOT BUILT. That is trap 64 -- which I wrote in this same pass -- committed
one commit after writing it.

G1. The registration obligation was ungated. impl-07 §7.4.1 says a generator
whose lowering introduces representation error MUST register a paired fidelity
generator. The plan said 8.7's gate 1 would check the pairing exists. 8.7 was
never touched -- `grep fidelity` returned nothing -- so the obligation was prose
with no build gate, the same shape as the glossary claiming a lint that did not
exist, which the certification fleet caught earlier this pass. Gate 1 now fails
the build on a missing pairing, and names the one exemption.

G2. Materialization was deleted from the open register and landed nowhere. The
plan said it "moves to Stage-4 codegen (4.2 §7.4)". 2.3 and 4.4 duly say it
belongs there; 4.2 §7.4 had never heard of it. Removing a problem from the open
list without landing it is worse than leaving it open -- it reads as solved. It
is now the fourth Stage-4 decision: the adjoint-tape rematerialize-vs-store
schedule, revolve-optimal on a chain, NP-hard on a general DAG, decided at
compile time.

  Worth the words it took: this is the one lowering that owes NO fidelity
  generator, because recomputing a value and reading a stored one give the same
  value. It trades memory against time and introduces no discrepancy. Saying so
  marks the boundary of the epsilon rule -- the rule is about VALUE, not COST --
  which the rule needed and did not have.

G3. One of the four load-bearing citations was missing in-page. tau_trunc rests
on the a-posteriori inexact-hypergradient result and nothing cited it. Ehrhardt &
Roberts, IMA J. Appl. Math. 89(1) 254-278 (2024), now cited at both sites, with
the bound it supports written out (||J^-1||*||r_stop||).

G4. A removed open item left no forwarding address. The verification says "every
removed item names where it went"; the gamma-hat entry had been deleted outright,
so a reader saw four items where there were five and no pointer -- while the
verifier gaps in the same file got struck through, dated, and told where they
went. The gamma-hat entry is restored in that same form, with each of the four
sub-questions pointing at its disposition.
