c0e345f 2026-07-22 Exact identity, measured error: close the gamma-hat / epsilon-equality holes
The four "unsolved CS problems" of arch-15 §15.4, plus the Stage-3
rewrite-soundness gap and the truncated-inner-solve gap found this month, were
never six problems. They were one collision seen six ways.

THE COLLISION. arch-20 §20.4 makes identity exact -- Address = SHA-256(canonical
bytes), with floats normalised "so numerically equal values always share an
address". Every object also carries an epsilon that combineTol composes. But `==`
(address) is an EQUIVALENCE relation and `~_eps` (numerical closeness) is a
TOLERANCE relation: not transitive, since ||a-b||<=eps and ||b-c||<=eps give only
2eps. A tolerance relation induces a covering by maximal cliques, not a partition
-- so no canonical representative, so nothing to hash, and hash-consing, Merkle
dedup and O(1) address equality die with it. The substrate needs a quotient and
the mathematics offers only a covering.

THE RULE, and it is the whole design:

  Identity is exact, always -- epsilon never enters the address. Any operation
  that can make the computed object differ from its exact counterpart emits a
  computable A-POSTERIORI estimate of that difference, as cert evidence.

`~_eps` is then never used as an identity relation, so its non-transitivity never
has to be repaired. Nothing branches at runtime -- the kernel computes a NUMBER,
and computing a number is not structural branching, so always-cheap survives.
Nothing is refused for being approximate; it is SCORED, and the consumer decides.
That is this project's own thesis -- score don't solve, evidence never verdicts --
finally applied to the oracle's own numerics instead of only to the physics. And
the gate gets WEAKER: "must be exact" becomes "must be able to estimate its own
error", which admits strictly more.

No new residual category. These are cert-only generators, so the 19-tag enum is
untouched and nothing pollutes the training loss -- a network must not be able to
reduce its loss by making the oracle's self-assessment optimistic. Every estimator
is a byproduct of work already done: a truncated SVD already computes the
discarded singular value, a truncated solve already computes its stopping residual.

WHAT THE RESEARCH SETTLED. Three faces were not open, and I had said otherwise:

  Rank drift. I claimed low-rank truncation blows up as 1/sigma_min. True of
  STANDARD integrators, false of projector-splitting (Lubich-Oseledets 2014) and
  BUG families, whose bounds are provably independent of small singular values
  (Kieri-Lubich-Walach 2016). The rank-adaptive BUG integrator
  (Ceruti-Kusch-Lubich 2022) preserves, up to a declared tolerance, the norm when
  the equation does, the energy for Hamiltonian systems, and monotone decrease in
  gradient flows -- trace, the L block and the M block, term for term.

  Truncated inner solves have computable a-posteriori error estimates. Not
  unbudgeted; unmeasured.

  Materialization was misfiled. Forcing vs deferring changes COST, not VALUE, so
  it carries no error term at all. It is revolve-style checkpointing: a Stage-4
  schedule over the adjoint tape.

  And the keystone, which I twice called open: e-graph rewriting under float
  semantics is solved by side conditions discharged by an e-class analysis.
  Herbie's rules were known-unsound for years and DELETING them made the tool
  useless on much of its own benchmark suite -- exactness-only is not a viable
  gate. egglog (Zhang et al., PLDI 2023) made them sound with an interval analysis
  composed with a not-equals analysis; the sound version ran FASTER and was more
  accurate on 104 of 289 benchmarks. Its equality stays exact over R with the
  interval facts alongside -- the same separation, arrived at independently.

SCORER-ONLY, and what it resolved. gamma-hat never evolves inside /physics. That
is not a new constraint: arch-02-libraries and the README already denied
trajectories while arch-15 §15.2 listed time-stepping as a gamma-hat write path.
Canon wins. Drift is EXPORTED, not dissolved, so the steppable-form manifest now
carries the encoding validity domain and names the BUG family whose guarantees
match its existing conserve|bound|monotone obligation map term for term.

The open register actually shrank: the four gamma-hat questions are gone, and
three of four verifier-soundness gaps are closed (rewrite contract; truncation
budget via tau_trunc; the Layer-1.25 validity radius, which turns out to BE an
estimator -- ||dx|| times a compile-time sensitivity coefficient, now two fields
on OneShotCert). The one that survives is stated sharply rather than deleted: the
adjoint gate validates the FORMULA's adjoint at registration, Stage 4 synthesizes
the COMPOSITION's adjoint later over a rewritten graph, and nothing revalidates
the second. The epsilon rule does not reach it, and saying so is the point.

Traps 63-65. Also stabilised calibrate.py's trap-numbering probe, which was keyed
on the last trap and would have gone stale on this very commit.

c283e8b 2026-07-21 Git archaeology: restore the reasoning the collapse destroyed — and retract one claim of mine
A read-only archaeology pass recovered all seven items from `e12fabd` (the last
commit carrying the deleted docs/ tree). Its most useful result was against me.

RETRACTION. When I fixed accuracy-ledger row 2's dangling "the declared mesh-σ
floor", I wrote that the floor is "per-composition, produced by the gate at
compile time". That was a reconstruction I found plausible, not something I
recovered, and I stated it as fact. The archaeology checked every commit: no
numeric floor was EVER written, and the forward reference has dangled since
2026-06-10 — the collapse did not break it. My fix replaced a dangling pointer
with an invention, which is worse, because the dangling pointer at least looked
wrong.

What is actually established, and now recorded: BTE transport (mu, kappa_e,
alpha_ii) needs ~50^3-equivalent k-sampling; the MVP's 8x8x8 / 29-IBZ mesh is
factor-2-grade for transport even with exact inputs; Stage-2 IBZ reduction helps
COST, not convergence, so interpolation is the only path off factor-2. The floor
itself is now an open item rather than a claim.

Restored from history:

  The per-material breakdown-field sigma split, with its derivation chain: a
  factor-2 in the Chynoweth prefactor propagates to ~10-20% in E_b, amplified by
  field non-uniformity, giving diamond +/-20% and GaN +/-15%; E_b enters BFOM
  cubed. Row 20 carried a blanket +/-15% -- GaN's number applied to everything --
  while still attributing the BFOM consequence to diamond, whose own +/-20% had
  been dropped. That mismatch also predates the collapse.

  C13 superlinearity. This one is a CSV-vs-canon contradiction the promotion
  itself introduced: elastic-tensors.csv says "linear Vegard (<=4.7% bowing; C13
  superlinear)" and the canon summary kept the "weak link" half while silently
  dropping "superlinear", so canon described plain Vegard while the CSV it
  summarizes said otherwise.

  Why AlN has no hole-mass row. With Delta_cr < 0 the topmost valence band is
  CH-like, so the GaN-shaped A-band entry is UNDEFINED for AlN -- the absent row
  is a refusal, not an oversight, and that reason was recorded nowhere. The
  archaeology also confirmed no numeric Delta_cr exists in any commit; the row
  says sign-only and says not to invent one.

  Provenance for the two values I re-seeded earlier: AlN E_BR 12-15.4 is Maeda
  JAP 129 185702 (2021); GaN mu_n 1265 was quoted as a COMPARISON against the
  1034 first-principles ceiling, not an independently cited measurement, and now
  says so with the pin marked outstanding.

Three hazards were live and unnamed:

  4.2's "Three rewrites, all exact and AD-safe" was flagged in 2026-06 and is
  still stated unqualified. It holds for those three and is not a property of the
  rewrite SYSTEM; past them, exactness needs positivity and branch-cut guards
  that no rule carries, and no rule-soundness contract exists.

  Gradient error under TRUNCATED inner solves is unbudgeted. tau_cond guards the
  fixed-point Jacobian's conditioning at registration; nothing budgets the error
  an SCF stopped at tolerance, or the L3 cycle stopped at its 5-iteration cap,
  introduces at runtime. Different things; only the first exists.

  G0W0 cost is not scope-tagged by cell size: T2 (<=10 s) is plausible at MVP
  scale and hours for V1 defect supercells, so the tier table is violated
  silently rather than refused. OneShotCert has no validity-radius field to hold
  the bound either.

Cross-cutting, and worth keeping: items 4, 5 and 7(iv) were already broken before
the collapse. f43cbb8 destroyed the only surviving copy of the REASONING but did
not create those gaps -- so restoring them is a fix, not a revert.

61e5c91 2026-07-21 Three physics findings from the fleet, each verified by arithmetic before landing
  H interstitial profile shift. The corpus said "~1 mm profile shift in 1000 h"
  for D(500 C) = 1e-13 cm2/s. sqrt(D*t) with t = 3.6e6 s is 6.0e-4 cm = 6 um.
  The stated figure was 170x high -- three unit prefixes -- and it is the number
  a reader would use to decide whether hydrogen redistribution matters at the
  device scale over a service life. At 6 um it matters at a gate; at 1 mm it
  would matter across a die.

  Clausius-Clapeyron, registry row 68, was signed `(dH, dV) -> dP/dT`. That is
  dimensionally impossible: dH/dV has units of pressure, dP/dT has pressure per
  kelvin. The relation is dP/dT = dH/(T*dV), so T is an input. Signature
  corrected and the reasoning recorded in the source cell.

  Diamond's conduction-band minimum is not at X. It sits on the Delta line along
  <100> at k ~ 0.76 of Gamma->X, so the indirect gap is Gamma-25' -> Delta-1.
  "X-point" is the common shorthand and is wrong in the way that matters here:
  the six-fold Delta valley degeneracy is what the effective-mass tensor and the
  intervalley v_sat row consume, and X would imply three.

All three came from the certification fleet. I checked each one myself before
acting -- the arithmetic for the first, dimensional analysis for the second,
band structure for the third -- rather than taking the finding on trust.

e95521c 2026-07-21 Make "calibrate before believing a clean run" executable
Trap 58 says a checker that finds nothing may simply not be looking, and tells
you to plant a defect of the class you claim is absent before citing a green run.
Until now that was a discipline I performed by hand, in bash, and threw away --
which means the next person inherits the rule without the means to follow it.

journal/tools/calibrate.py runs 28 probes, one per check across both checkers. It
copies the corpus to a temp dir, plants one defect, runs the checker, asserts the
expected message appears, restores, and moves on. Nothing in the repo is touched.

Two failure modes, both reported:
  MISSED -- the checker did not fire. A hole in the checker.
  STALE  -- the planted text no longer exists in the corpus, so the probe has
            silently stopped testing anything. A hole in the probe list, which
            is the same failure one level up and the reason this is not just a
            list of greps.

It also refuses to run if the baseline is not clean, because a probe against a
dirty baseline proves nothing.

Current: 28/28 fired, 0 missed, 0 stale.

This session's two checker holes -- apparatus skipping every unprefixed id and
every dated anchor, seams' ASCII-only formula capture -- were both green the
whole time they were broken. Each now has a probe.

a5b5a96 2026-07-21 Check both directions of edge symmetry, and the registry's row pointers
Edge symmetry was checked one way only: A depends-on B implied B must list A,
but a `referenced-by` entry with no matching `depends-on` passed. That is the
direction a stale entry survives in -- delete a citation, remove the depends-on,
and the reverse entry stays -- and `referenced-by` is the field a reader follows
to find who consumes a page. Now checked both ways. Currently zero orphans.

The registry's `Depends on` column carries `name (row N)` pointers, and that
column is the machine-readable edge list a code generator would build the DAG
from. Nothing verified that the name and the number agree. A pointer naming the
wrong row builds the wrong DAG silently; a rename leaves it naming nothing. I
checked these by hand while normalizing them earlier this session and found four
mismatches -- which is exactly why it should not be a hand check.

silently repoints every citation to it -- and neither anchor harvester sees a

6.9 called the Path column retired on the grounds that the always-cheap reframe

    a legitimate model gap as a bug -- is DEFINED by the distinction;

Three verifier-soundness gaps are now NAMED in the open register rather than
left absent, because an absent check reads exactly like a passing one:

  1. no post-registration adjoint-drift monitoring -- the gate runs once, and
     nothing re-runs it when a Stage-3 rewrite or a compression-plan change
     alters the graph the adjoint was synthesized against;
  2. no Stage-3 rewrite-soundness contract -- trap 50 states the rule an evolver

previous commit and is recorded as closed.

tier is owned. It was implicit in the design and load-bearing -- a frozen
dressing contributes no gradient, so gap-versus-strain reaches the loss only
through row 63's deformation potential, never through the frozen G0W0 shift --
and the 2026-06-09 founding audit flagged that it was nowhere written down. The
error term it costs (dressing staleness) already existed; the policy that
explains it did not.

4c1fb93 2026-07-21 Fix the D2/D3 line, and the three fixed-point rows sitting on the wrong side
Correction to the previous commit's message: it claimed "19 more derived graph
edges" for that pass. It was 10. The 19 belonged to the pass before it. Nothing
in the corpus carried the wrong number -- only the message -- but the whole point
of trap 58 is not to let a count go unchecked because it looked plausible.

A systematic sweep of all 132 rows against the tag semantics -- rather than
against the skeptics' finding list -- turned up three rows whose output is a
converged fixed point but which were tagged D2:

  5   fermi-level-charge-neutral      E_F is the root of charge neutrality
  13  SCPH-self-consistent-phonons    omega_renorm is the converged solution
  36  self-consistent-charge-balance  E_F* over coupled defect and carrier populations

Retagging them exposed a defect in the vocabulary itself. D2 and D3 were written
as alternatives, but they answer different questions: D2 says the adjoint is
GATED at registration, D3 says the adjoint is an IMPLICIT-FUNCTION solve. Under
the old reading D3 was strictly WEAKER than D2 -- a fixed-point row could avoid
the gate by being tagged more precisely -- which is backwards, because canon
already names the near-singular Jacobian as D3's characteristic failure mode.

So D3 is now a refinement of D2, not an alternative: every D3 row runs the same
vJp-vs-JvP gate, plus a conditioning check on the fixed-point Jacobian at the
same sampled points (tau_cond, added to the ledger).

That second gate is not decoration. The vJp-vs-JvP check CANNOT see a
conditioning failure, because both sides solve against the same bad Jacobian and
agree -- on a gradient that is large and wrong. It is the mirror image of the
spurious pass in trap 52, where they agree because the true gradient is zero.
And the rows it bites are the corpus's own subject matter: charge neutrality in a
wide-gap intrinsic semiconductor has the flattest dF/dE_F there is, and rows 5
and 36 sit on exactly that. Trap 60.

Also swept and cleared, with the reasoning recorded rather than re-derived later:
rows 56/57 (mixed output -- the convention covers them), 111 (NRT is
piecewise-linear in a real, kinks are an exception set, not a discrete output),
72/125/134 (named "balance" but explicit closed forms), 112/124 (the hull is an
INPUT to 124; its non-smoothness is declared on row 67 where it lives).

Tally: D0 1 / DN 6 / D1 91 / D2 23 / D3 5 / D4 6.

Ten registry rows were tagged or named wrongly. Each is corrected with the

  12  D3 -> D1   gamma_lambda is a finite difference of input frequencies, not a

of canon. Both 7.2 and 4.4 called D3 a "downgrade" target, which is the retired

Naming is addressing, so renames now leave forwarding addresses:
physics/library/formulas/retired-names.csv maps 62 retired names to what they
landed as, derived from git history and corroborated against each target row's
a.k.a. text. `makov-payne-correction` maps to two rows -- it named both 31 and
89 before the split, which is why it was renamed.

The checkers were part of the defect, not the remedy:

  - apparatus only validated bracketed ids matching arch|impl|mvp|deriv, so
    [product], [traps], [conventions] and every other unprefixed id went
    unchecked -- including one dangling [instructions].
  - seams captured `formula =` arguments with an ASCII-only class, so an
    argument written with a Greek sigma was invisible.
  - the depends-on edge criterion was documented as derivable but derived by
    nobody. It is now machine-derived over the citable body (prose minus code
    fences, changelogs and banners); it found 19 missing edges.
  - no acyclicity check, deliberately, and now stated: the page graph is one
    large SCC by design and nothing may read a closure over it as layering.
  - journal/instructions.md -- the file an agent reads FIRST -- was checked by
    nothing and was badly stale: it advertised a deleted build_book.py, a
    collapsed docs/ tree, and the superseded authority order. Rewritten, and
    seams now covers it.

Calibration: 15 planted defects, one per check, 15 fired. Two of my own new
checks missed on the first pass (retired-name matching was case-sensitive;
near-miss matching required a lowercase initial) and were fixed before this
result was believed. Case-insensitive matching then found five more real sites.

95b8a06 2026-07-21 Declare the unregistered-formula gap instead of registering on thin provenance
18 formula names invoked by impl-06-compositions are not registry rows, so
that page's closed-vocabulary claim was false. Rather than register them to
make a checker go quiet, the gap is now declared, tracked, and machine-checked.

Why not register: a registry row must be defensible against a literature
citation. About half of these are modelling choices with real literature
behind them (four distinct hardness models, the HTST and jump-diffusivity
prefactor conventions, turnover frequency), and registering those on invented
provenance would put unsourced rows into the one artifact whose entire
discipline is that unsourced values get refused (traps 19).

What landed:
  - impl-06-compositions declares the names in `unregistered-formulas`
    frontmatter, and both of its false completeness claims are corrected.
    The page now states the gap and splits it into the two tranches that
    need different work: definitional (transcription plus tag assignment)
    versus needs-sourcing (real literature).
  - seams distinguishes DECLARED from UNDECLARED. A declared name is tracked;
    a new unregistered name still fails loudly. That is the same distinction
    the corpus already makes for GAP rows and cert-refusals.
  - inline mathematics is now its own finding class rather than being
    miscounted as a naming gap. Four invocations are raw expressions, which
    impl-04-formulas forbids outright.
  - the braced-alternation parse was wrong: `{chen | teter | tian |
    mazhnik-oganov}-hardness` was being read as the single token "chen".
    It now expands to all four names.

seams: 16 formula-arg findings -> 0; 2 inline-math remain, correctly classified.

00177b2 2026-07-21 Redesign the differentiability vocabulary; retag 19 rows
The old D0 conflated two opposite answers to the one question the tag exists
to answer. "A pure read, whose adjoint is the identity" and "an integer
output, which has no derivative at all" both sat at D0 — and the conflation
was load-bearing, not cosmetic: impl-11-verification exempts D0 from
registering an adjoint, so eleven *smooth analytic* rows were set to ship
with no gradient at all. Among them the alloy-lattice interpolation, which
is linear in composition, and which product.md explicitly promises is
"directly optimizable through the baked gradients".

New vocabulary, canonical in impl-04-formulas:

  D0  pure read — adjoint is the identity, no synthesis
  DX  no useful derivative — integer / categorical / set-valued  [NEW]
  D1  smooth; gradient available directly, no registration gate
  D2  adjoint required, validated at registration (vJp-vs-JvP, tau_adj)
  D3  implicit-function adjoint through a converged fixed point
  D4  relaxed — declared smooth relaxation, bias enters combineTol

Retag: 21 D0 rows split 11 -> D1 (the smooth ones that were adjoint-less),
8 -> DX (topology invariants and discrete classifications), 2 stay D0 (the
genuine table/cache reads). New tally D0 2 / DX 8 / D1 91 / D2 25 / D3 3 /
D4 3.

Also fixed the reason three incompatible legends could coexist: the legend
was *stated* in three places. 6.9-formula-registry now links to
impl-04-formulas instead of restating it, and the glossary's canonical
pointer moved to the actual owner. One fact, one home.

Propagated to the stated histogram in 4.4-computational-overview and the
adjoint-exemption rule in 8.7-verification, which now reads "D0 and DX
register without an adjoint, and D1 without a gate".

apparatus OK — 58 pages, 99 canonical topics, graph symmetric, hashes fresh.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

e12fabd 2026-07-21 Add the book apparatus: contents, index, instructions, and its checker
tools/apparatus.py replaces the deleted assemble.py. Its predecessor emitted
monoliths that restated the tree; this one emits navigation and enforces the
invariants that make the tree trustworthy.

Generated at the root:
  contents.md   chapters -> pages in reading order
  index.md      canonical topic -> the one page that owns it

Checked (exit 1 on failure), currently green at 56 pages / 97 topics:
  1. frontmatter present, parseable, required fields
  2. `id` globally unique
  3. `canonical-for` globally unique   <- the anti-drift invariant
  4. depends-on / referenced-by symmetric
  5. every [id] reference in prose resolves
  6. content-hash matches the body

The hash is tool-maintained: regenerating restamps it, --check verifies it.
That asymmetry is deliberate — a stale hash means a page was hand-edited
without regenerating, which is exactly what would break working-copy
staleness detection. Restamping caught 7 pages where the migration hashed
the body before normalizing leading whitespace.

instructions.md tells a fresh agent how to go book -> graph (read contents,
find the owning page in the index, follow depends-on to closure) and
graph -> book (find the topic's owner, edit there, never state a fact
twice, restamp). It also states the working-copy contract: a copy without
per-block `id` provenance cannot be written back mechanically.

glossary.md moved to the root as apparatus. assemble.py and manifest.yaml
deleted — their job died with the monoliths. lint.py and seams.py relocated
to tools/; they still target the old tree layout and are pending retarget.

9c45ccb 2026-07-21 Migrate corpus into the book: 56 pages across 11 chapters
Structure now matches the agreed target:

    pages/<chapter>.<n>-<slug>.md      47 canon pages
    pages/appendix/...                  9 derivation pages (supporting)

Chaptered by concern, oracle-dominant — chapters 2-6 are the contiguous
oracle block (inputs/state, laws/residuals, pipeline/compilation,
certification/applicability, vocabularies/registry).

Identity model, three jobs three mechanisms:
  - stable address = the page `id`, preserved verbatim from existing
    frontmatter. This is why the migration is non-destructive to the
    citation graph: every [arch-07-pipeline] reference and every
    `arch-12 §12.0.3` coordinate still resolves. Only path-based
    citations need repair.
  - display tag    = <chapter>.<n>, disposable, regenerated freely,
    never cited.
  - content-hash   = sha256 of the body, stamped per page. Staleness
    detection for working copies.

`authority` (canon | supporting) so the authority order promoted in
10.1-conventions is machine-readable rather than prose-only.

ec52314 2026-06-08 Close arch-18 §1: implementation-language decision (polyglot Haskell/Julia + GAP/Lean)
Resolve the single blocking open decision via two research rounds + an adversarial

- Haskell compiler-host for Stages 1–4 + the arch-20 substrate (emits the kernel):
  GADTs/DataKinds/type-families type the op-indexed Merkle DAG and the
  SymbolicTensorOps operad at compile time; hegg for the Stage-3 e-graph;
  GHC.Generics-derived §20.4 serializer; our own AD drives Stage-4 adjoints.

  composition, so the hot loop is native Julia with no per-sample FFI; Julia owns
  the optional GPU codegen.
- GAP (offline) for Stage-2/2.5 group-theory tables; Lean 4 (offline) for §20.4
  pre-hash encoding injectivity + EvidenceOps/GroupOps/ROBDD algebraic laws.
- CPU-dominant compiler; GPU a Stage-5-only per-composition accelerator (small
  irrep blocks are a GPU anti-pattern). Net win conditional on a Stage-4→Stage-5
  differential golden test; single-host (Julia-only / Haskell-only) is the fallback.

(its tracer owns differentiation, conflicting with bring-your-own adjoint synthesis).

    Sampleable AND non-local witnesses like Onsager involution.

bc7bd60 2026-05-28 Layer 0 RESHAPE: three-axis / four-typeclass decomposition
Replaces the flat four-interface sketch (Scalar / FieldOnGrid / Tensor /
Response as siblings) with three orthogonal axes captured by four
typeclasses. The earlier sketch braided two axes (domain-shape +
analytic-constraint) into one list and left ~12 registry rows (integer
invariants, classification groups, holonomy spectra, polyhedra, hulls)
with no typeclass home.

New Layer 0 alphabet (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN §11):

  Quantity              -- units, tolerance, change-of-units
  Sampleable            -- function-on-domain (shape axis)
    + Integrable        -- à-la-carte capability
    + Differentiable    -- à-la-carte capability
    + Restrictable      -- à-la-carte capability
  HasAnalyticStructure  -- witness-bearing constraint (causality,
                           hermiticity, convexity, KK-pairing)
  DiscreteStructure     -- missing fourth axis: combinatorial outputs

Old names (Scalar, FieldOnGrid, Tensor, Response) preserved as type
aliases over the new typeclasses. Downstream prose reads unchanged.

Cert obligations now map mechanically onto axes (IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN
§13.1): obligation-1 → Sampleable+GroupAction; obligation-2 →
Quantity ordering; obligation-3 → HasAnalyticStructure; obligation-5
→ Integrable; obligation-6 → Quantity.approxEq on shared Sampleable
domain; obligation-7 → Morphism in DiscreteStructure.

Topology atlas outputs (X_BS group, integer invariants, holonomy
spectrum, boundary-mode-multiplicity lookup) are first-class
DiscreteStructure instances (§28.4); the boundary-correspondence cert
obligation is literally a Morphism in DiscreteStructure, not a stub.

Two worked examples through the new alphabet in §15.0:
  - DielectricFunction (continuous, causal, integrable, differentiable)
  - ChernNumber (the discrete invariant the old vocabulary had no
    honest home for)
